# EPC-022: the 4.0.0 milestone fixes

## Summary

The 4.0.0 release plan addresses issues filed by this repository and
by adopters. The original 2026-10-12 scope covered 26 issues against
CodeFlow 3.0.0 and 3.1.0. The combined scope covers `codeflow ci`
verdicts that depend on how a range is spelled, planning and exemption pull
requests that cannot be classified, guards that miss commands, writers and
history reads that can be redirected in a local clone, CodeFlow's own pushes
using a personal credential, managed CI steps an adopter must edit by hand,
a landing mode, tests that touch an enclosing repository, and one
post-release evaluation. This epic fixes or settles each of them for the
4.0.0 release, which the operator renumbered from 3.2.0 on 2026-10-28
(Version relabel amendment below).

It serves every adopter and contributor who runs `codeflow ci`, the hooks,
the guards and the managed CI. Done means each task below has landed on
`integration/EPC-022-the-3-2-0-milestone-fixes` with its cross-lineage
review, the integrated body passes the full gate and the journey below on
the installed candidate, and its pull request to `main` is ready for a
human merge.

Source: the issue audit of 2026-10-12 against v3.1.0 (`698cc8789`), which
read every issue and its comments and the code each one names, and the
four issues added to the milestone the same day (149 to 152).

Amended 2026-10-13 after the 3.1.1 triage (workspace `RELEASE-PLAN.md`,
"After 3.1.1 (triage of 2026-10-13)") and the adopter feature design the
operator approved that day: seven new tasks for issues 156, 161, 175 and
176, the narrow binding defect of issue 167, the pin-lowering fix the design
found, and the stale release-integration workflow; TSK-303, the sample
adopter gates run TSK-097 deferred; and new criteria in TSK-275 (issue
165), TSK-291 (issue 151's comment) and TSK-286 (issue 102's comments). The
line took 3.1.1 through PR 184, the merge of `main` (4226a9408) as
4a16a43d6, on 2026-10-14. The adopter features themselves (issues 164,
166, 167 parts A to D, 168 to 172 and 174) are EPC-023, a separate epic
whose tasks land on this line too (operator decision of 2026-10-14).

Amended 2026-10-15 with four follow-ups found while building and
reviewing tasks on this line: TSK-326 (guards allow git operands and
environment supplied as data, a high finding of the TSK-283 security
reviews that reproduces on 3.1.0, with a low bracket-arithmetic gap folded
in), TSK-327 (the pre-push destination query still reads local
configuration, history inputs and the pushing environment's ssh command,
from TSK-304's Follow-ups), TSK-328 (a presentation test that failed
once under load) and TSK-329 (a gate target that can start before the
binary it runs exists). They add criteria AC-23 to AC-26 below.

Amended 2026-10-16 with four more follow-ups: TSK-330 (every hook refuses
every command while a settings file holds merge conflict markers, so an
agent cannot help resolve the conflict; reproduced on 3.1.0), TSK-331
(essential gate runs take no machine lock and time each other out),
TSK-332 (a killed docs portal run leaves a workflow lease that blocks
builds for four hours) and TSK-333 (version pins compared and written
with two different parsers, finding N2 of the TSK-309 review). They add
criteria AC-27 to AC-30 below. The same amendment records two
reassignments: Codex built TSK-329 (PR 205) and TSK-309 (PR 207), whose
records named a Claude builder, so their reviewers became Claude
(Fable 5.1 at high).

Amended 2026-10-17 with the accepted release triage: credential protection,
hosted policy authority, Codex startup, first adoption and completion
provenance; restored writer, performance, YAML and qualification plans;
and the secret-scan migration and revision-walk repairs. The operator
assigned drafting to Codex. Claude Fable 5.1 approved the whole amendment
in round two on 2026-10-17 at
`0c62644d1ae3858168694328c2dc26f678f30a46`; verdict file:
`../triage-3-2-0/target/review-224-fable-02.md`.
Design intent is N/A for these planning records. The primary and TSK-292 builder
settled its held-filter journey as push or full-history scanning, retaining
the existing PR base-only trust model.

## Acceptance Criteria

- AC-1 When `codeflow ci` judges a range, its verdict shall not depend on
  how the base is spelled or where the command runs, and a planning
  amendment that updates the document index or an exemption-only pull
  request into the base shall each get a class; evidence: TSK-275, TSK-276
  and TSK-277.
- AC-2 When a closed record holds text a project must remove, a reviewed
  record-only change shall replace it while every result, the reviewed
  commit and the review provenance stay, and the acceptance waiver shall be
  documented; evidence: TSK-278.
- AC-3 Before a push, an author shall get the release-impact verdict hosted
  CI gives, and a new policy key shall follow one written placement rule
  that a check holds; evidence: TSK-279 and TSK-280.
- AC-4 No CodeFlow writer or judging history read shall be redirected by a
  link at a temporary name, a graft, a replace ref or a shallow boundary in
  a local clone; evidence: TSK-281 and TSK-287.
- AC-5 The guards shall judge what a command will actually do: git's
  effective configuration, files that run code in a later shell or login,
  package-manager script shells, and deletion inputs by each native tool's
  grammar. They shall refuse the alias environment of issue 98, the PATH,
  `environment.d` and login-item writes, the package-manager script-shell
  settings and the deletion bypasses. A write to a file that git's
  configuration includes follows TSK-283 AC-4: the write is refused, or
  `codeflow doctor` reports the writable include and what the guard cannot
  see; evidence: TSK-283, TSK-284, TSK-285 and TSK-286.
- AC-6 The test suite shall never write outside its own temporary folders
  or hang on a small pipe buffer, whatever `TMPDIR` is; evidence: TSK-282.
- AC-9 When a project configures a non-personal credential, every push
  CodeFlow makes for it shall use that credential and never the ambient
  one, and the setting shall be one a pull request cannot change for its
  own pushes; evidence: TSK-291.
- AC-10 A project shall refuse inline `gitleaks:allow` and route
  dependency-audit exceptions through its own reviewed check, both read
  from the trusted commit, with no local edit to the managed workflow;
  evidence: TSK-292 and TSK-294.
- AC-11 `codeflow integrate` shall land a reviewed head with a
  no-fast-forward merge on request, keeping the reviewed commit and its
  acceptance binding unchanged; evidence: TSK-293.
- AC-12 (journey) On the installed combined candidate, a fresh
  `codeflow init --standard` project and a project updated from 3.1.0 shall
  pass the full gate, the guards shall refuse each planted case these tasks
  add except the include-file case, which the journey plants and closes the
  way TSK-283 chose (refused, or reported by doctor with what the guard
  cannot see), a configured push credential shall carry `codeflow task new`'s
  reservation, and `codeflow ci` shall give one verdict for a sync merge
  spelled both ways; evidence: the final integration run, with every task
  reviewed by the other lineage, and the pull request to `main` prepared
  for a human merge.
- AC-14 When `git.test_gate_on_push` is `block`, a pushed ref whose tree
  the hook cannot check (dirty tracked files, a ref other than `HEAD`,
  several refs, a sparse or incomplete checkout, a submodule mismatch)
  shall be checked on the pushed commit or refused, never passed with a
  note; evidence: TSK-304.
- AC-15 When the destination does not answer the default-branch query,
  the run shall still refuse, under one rule that names the cause and the
  network, never under a criteria or records rule; evidence: TSK-305.
- AC-16 When a line replaced a task version that `main` inherited from it
  unchanged, the line's version shall be the criteria authority, while
  concurrent amendments, reverts, merge resolutions and unreadable history
  still give no answer; evidence: TSK-306.
  - Amended 2026-10-28: one bounded exception, once the operator approves it in TSK-350: for a task's own criteria change in a range whose confirmed `--into` target is the integration line the task declares, the default branch's tip passed as `--policy-from` is not a criteria point, provided the line passes the trusted-line binding on either entry point; concurrent amendments among the remaining points still give no answer and main stays the policy and commit-history authority. The line-to-main landing does not check competing copies automatically today (an epic line range skips the frozen-criteria comparison); the proposal adds a report there, at the level the operator decides (TSK-350 AC-6, AC-8 and AC-9).
- AC-17 A direct commit on an epic line that a landed `line_adoptions`
  entry adopts shall not refuse the acceptance binding of tasks landed
  before it, and a forged or unlanded entry shall still refuse; evidence:
  TSK-308.
- AC-18 An acceptance file without its top-level key shall be refused by
  the file's name, not the record's; evidence: TSK-307.
- AC-19 `codeflow update` run by a binary older than the project's pin
  shall refuse and change nothing; evidence: TSK-309.
- AC-21 No scheduled or per-landing workflow of this repository shall
  target a release branch that does not exist or that no open
  release-integration task owns; evidence: TSK-310.
- AC-22 (journey) On the installed combined candidate, a dirty-tree push
  of a commit that fails the push set shall be refused, a task branch on a
  line that replaced `main`'s inherited task version shall push cleanly,
  and an adopted direct commit shall leave earlier bindings intact;
  evidence: the journeys of TSK-304, TSK-306 and TSK-308 rerun in the
  final integration run.
- AC-23 When a git ref, remote, environment assignment or program in a
  command comes from a variable assigned from data or from `xargs`,
  git-guard and exec-guard shall judge it as unresolved and refuse it; a
  ref built with bracket arithmetic shall be resolved as the shell will and
  judged against policy by git-guard, so a protected ref is refused and an
  unprotected one passes; evidence: TSK-326.
- AC-24 The pre-push destination query shall not be redirected by on-disk
  URL rewriting, nor read its range base or ssh transport from state the
  hook did not choose, and a setting it cannot isolate shall be handled as
  an unchecked destination; evidence: TSK-327.
- AC-25 The presentation close and show tests that scan other processes
  shall pass reliably under host load, with the cause of the failure of the
  TSK-283 gate run found; evidence: TSK-328.
- AC-26 (journey) The `model-eval-kit` gate target shall succeed in each
  supported gate mode, with only its declared prerequisites and from an
  empty artifact directory, whatever order the scheduler picks, and a gate
  target that runs a binary under `target/` shall declare the target that
  produces it; evidence: TSK-329.
- AC-27 While the index holds unmerged entries for `.codeflow/project.toml`
  or `.codeflow/policy.json`, a hook's otherwise failing local read of that
  file shall be replaced by the copy committed at `HEAD`, with authority
  selection, the stricter composition with fetched lines, protected refs,
  root-branch selection and levels unchanged, so the commands that resolve
  or abandon the conflict get the ordinary effective-policy verdict and no
  guard is weaker; an unreadable file with no unmerged entry, or an
  unreadable authority, still refuses and never falls back to defaults;
  evidence: TSK-330.
- AC-28 On one machine, an essential or full `codeflow test` run shall
  never overlap another essential or full run, and the second shall refuse
  naming the holder and its mode; a quick run and the pre-push gate shall
  take the same lock for their heavy targets, with the default class of
  an undeclared quick target and the wait bound set by the project, and
  the lock holder shall be readable; evidence: TSK-331.
  - Amended 2026-10-22: widened for issue #276 (TSK-331 AC-12 and AC-13);
    the earlier text said quick runs and the pre-push gate take no lock,
    which the 2026-10-17 amendment had already changed for heavy targets.
- AC-29 A docs portal workflow lease whose workflow on the same host has
  stopped (its coordinating process and every writer it started) shall be
  reclaimed at once with a message; a live coordinator or a live writer
  shall still block, including a writer that outlives a killed
  coordinator, and a lease whose coordinator or writers cannot be checked
  shall keep the four-hour age rule; evidence: TSK-332.
- AC-61 Every documented policy key shall have a reader or be reported as
  unsupported, and `security.script_bypass`, `security.sandbox_retry` and
  `security.sandbox_retry_allow` shall no longer be shipped or documented
  as controls; evidence: TSK-363.
- AC-62 A substituted hook binary that answers the capability handshake
  shall be refused at every hook entry point with the repository
  unchanged, and integrate-token and human-override use shall be recorded;
  evidence: TSK-364, building on TSK-324's in-session refusal.
- AC-68 (journey) On the final candidate, an impostor hook binary shall
  be refused with a staged synthetic secret and leave the repository
  unchanged; evidence: the final integration run.
- AC-30 Every comparison of versions and every writer of a pin shall use
  one parser, and no caller shall order an unparseable version as a
  string; evidence: TSK-333.
- AC-48 When a gate run on a task branch names no base, it shall select
  against the merge base with the task's declared line and skip a target
  only when it is `content` class and recorded evidence covers its exact
  command, tools, platform, relevant environment, membership, producer
  outputs and unchanged inputs, falling back to running the target, with
  the reason named, whenever any of that is unproven; a landing candidate's
  gate shall always execute every target; evidence: TSK-352.
- AC-49 This repository's gate targets shall declare the inputs they read,
  so a records-only or docs-only change runs only the checks that read
  those paths, while hook, guard, policy, scaffold and Rust changes run
  every relevant check; evidence: TSK-353's path-to-target matrix.
  - Amended 2026-10-28: the narrowing takes effect only where TSK-1044 adopts a protected recorder; without one, every requested target runs with the reason and the narrowed runs are deferred (TSK-353 AC-3, AC-5, AC-6).
- AC-50 Essential Rust verification shall run under nextest with explicit
  doctests and the same test membership as `cargo test --workspace`;
  evidence: TSK-354.
- AC-51 A completed target execution whose evidence matches the current
  check shall be reused and name the run that executed it, while
  history-dependent checks and every pre-push protection run live, and a
  run with any reused target never counts as a complete gate; evidence:
  TSK-355.
  - Amended 2026-10-28: reuse runs in explicit gate runs, never on the push (TSK-449), and earns credit only from executions attested through TSK-1044's trust mechanism; with no protected recorder, reuse from local records is not enabled and every requested target executes (TSK-355 AC-7 to AC-10, after TSK-1044).
- AC-52 The full gate shall keep every test case while repeated identical
  fixture work runs once and exhaustive matrices are assigned to it
  explicitly, and its serial-group time shall drop; evidence: TSK-356.
- AC-53 Heavy targets inside one gate shall share one machine worker
  budget, this repository's Cargo producers shall not wait on each other's
  build directory lock, and heavy work started elsewhere through CodeFlow
  (heavy quick targets, the quick-to-essential fallback and the sanctioned
  wrapper for direct heavy commands) shall wait or refuse while a landing
  gate holds the machine; evidence: TSK-331 AC-2, AC-8, AC-9 and AC-10.
- AC-54 This repository's Rust build profiles and test layout shall cut
  target disk use and build or test time without a new tool, each change
  kept only when its measurement meets its stated criterion, and the
  compiler cache verdict shall be recorded with its revisit event;
  evidence: TSK-357.
- AC-55 A pull request into an integration line shall get hosted Linux and
  Windows verdicts before it lands once it is ready for review, a draft
  shall not start those jobs, and ordinary landings on a line shall wait
  until its current tip has green hosted verdicts, with only the repair and
  evidence-producing work allowed to land meanwhile; once the line is
  green, a ruleset shall require both hosted checks on `integration/*`;
  evidence: TSK-358 and TSK-360.
- AC-56 A push shall reuse a hosted verdict only from the run that tested
  the exact merged pull request into the exact destination, on the pushed
  tree, under the same workflow content and job contract, with the secret
  scan, canary and security review still live, or the work shall record
  that no such pair occurs; evidence: TSK-359.
- AC-57 Builders shall cite targeted tests and the quick gate instead of
  routine whole essential or full gates, one full gate shall run on each
  landing candidate, and both this repository's contract and the shipped
  pipeline default shall say so; evidence: TSK-360 and TSK-370.
- AC-58 (journey) After TSK-352 to TSK-360, TSK-370 and the amended TSK-331
  land, a records-only task branch cut from the line shall push with a
  quick gate that runs only the checks that read records and reuses
  unchanged results, its ready pull request shall get green hosted Linux
  and Windows verdicts before landing, the landing push shall reuse that
  verdict where the tree is identical, and the landing candidate's full
  gate shall still run every target; the elapsed times of the quick run,
  the full gate and the hosted runs shall be recorded before and after;
  evidence: one recorded run of that path on the line.
- AC-31 Supported cloud and registry credential stores, helper output and raw credential variables shall be denied in the supported lanes while credentialless tools remain usable. Evidence: TSK-334's synthetic fixtures and fresh/update native journeys.
- AC-32 Bare, relative and absolute hard-secret paths shall receive equivalent shell verdicts, with documented carve-outs preserved. Evidence: TSK-335's matcher matrix and installed hook journey.
- AC-33 Only trusted authority and the full required contract shall emit the required hosted policy verdict; shipped checkouts shall leave no persisted token available to later repository code. Evidence: TSK-336's actual GitHub check-state and fresh/update workflow journeys.
  - Amended 2026-10-29: made exact by the content policy amendment: trusted authority is the default branch's code at one SHA for every pull request whatever its target, the required policy, secret-scan and release-impact checks on the default branch are created by a dedicated GitHub App the agent cannot hold (Mode A), and lines require the `trusted:` names that only the default branch's job emits (TSK-336 AC-1, AC-9 to AC-12).
  - Amended 2026-10-30: merge admission and the verdicts built on it accept only a gates verdict from the default branch's `codeflow-ci` workflow and verdict-deciding inputs at the authority SHA, with the head's code as the candidate under test; a head's or a line's copy never decides admission (TSK-336 AC-14, after the Grok review of PR 396).
- AC-34 Prescribed Codex launches shall reach an authenticated prompt with the intended effective guard or builder boundary on the supported release. Evidence: TSK-337's native startup and permission canaries. A builder seat shall be able to take the gate lock and record its run (`~/.codeflow/locks`, `~/.codeflow/gate-runs`), and `codeflow doctor` shall report the Codex profiles' grants.
  - Amended 2026-10-22: widened for issue #254 (TSK-337 AC-5 and AC-6).
- AC-35 An existing repository whose main never contained CodeFlow policy shall adopt through a guarded task PR, push and completion. Removed, unreadable and unverifiable policy histories shall remain refused. Evidence: TSK-338's adoption journey and negative controls.
- AC-36 Imported unchanged completions shall retain their proven home landing and accepted criteria, while receiving-line changes remain accountable. Evidence: TSK-339's main, sibling-line and task-branch matrix and tamper controls.
- AC-37 Generated blocker changes, repeated task completion and subsequent epic Closeout shall have an ordinary command sequence accepted locally and for the final range. Evidence: TSK-340's repeated-closeout journey, provenance and tamper controls. A reopened task's records-only waiver amendment inside its own range shall be accepted as the never-reopened path accepts it, or the refusal shall name the planning-commit requirement.

  (AC-8).
- AC-197 When a task branch merges in another line that carries a
  completed task, through either parent, and then adds its own commits,
  `codeflow ci` shall bind that completion where its own line introduced
  it, judged as that line judges it, and shall not refuse the task
  branch's later commits on it, and two completions made in one task
  range shall not refuse each other's status and Closeout; a foreign
  record or acceptance block that the merge or a later commit changes,
  a byte-identical record that would not bind on its own line, and any
  other record change after a reviewed commit stay refused; a completion
  the range itself adds, changes or reopens stays bound at the head, and
  a later non-planning commit on it stays refused, which does not
  refuse the other completing task's status and Closeout; evidence:
  TSK-1123.
  Added 2026-10-31 by the CI merged completions amendment (issue #455).
- AC-198 When a sync merge carries a `main` task's record byte for byte as
  `main`'s tip holds it, `codeflow ci --branch <line>` shall judge it as
  `main`'s own content, taking `main`'s tip as its criteria authority,
  and give the verdict pre-push gives, while a record edited by one
  byte, an unreadable default branch or blob and a line-side reopen stay
  refused, and status steps stay judged as before; evidence: TSK-1124.
  Added 2026-10-31 by the same amendment (issue #456).

## Out of scope

- The 3.1.1 patch, cut from `main` by the operator's decision of
  2026-10-12, on the GitHub milestone "3.1.1"
  (https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/milestone/3): issues 147
  (https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/issues/147, the sed `w` false
  positive, TSK-290), 148
  (https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/issues/148, actions pinned by tag)
  and 153 (https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/issues/153, pre-push and a
  record main does not hold). TSK-290 was first planned here and moved to
  3.1.1; its id stays reserved and its record lands on `main` with its fix.
  This line takes 3.1.1 when it merges `main`. TSK-286 builds on TSK-290
  in prose, not in `depends_on`. 3.1.1 shipped on 2026-10-13; the line
  merged `main` at 4226a9408 through PR 184 (4a16a43d6) on 2026-10-14.
- Ownership of the adopter features: EPC-023 (issues 164, 166, 167 parts
  A to D, 168 to 172 and 174) is a separate epic with its own outcomes and
  evidence. By the operator's decision of 2026-10-14, replacing its own
  line, its tasks land on this line, so this exclusion is of epic
  ownership, not of the shared branch. Its readiness milestones on this
  line: S1 when TSK-280, TSK-283, TSK-284, TSK-285, TSK-287 and TSK-309
  have landed (EPC-023 implementation starts only after S1); S2 when
  TSK-276, TSK-286, TSK-292, TSK-306 and TSK-308 have; S3 when TSK-293 has.
  EPC-023 tasks name this epic's tasks in `depends_on`; no task here
  depends on EPC-023. Both epics are proven on the line's final combined
  candidate, and the line's pull request to `main` carries `Task: EPC-022`
  and describes both epics' outcomes and evidence.
  Replaced on 2026-10-18: EPC-023 is the 4.1.0 epic, its remaining tasks
  do not land here before 4.0.0 ships, and TSK-322 to TSK-325 moved into
  this epic (Release scope amendment below). The line's pull request to
  `main` describes this epic's outcomes and evidence.
- The baseline replacement (issue 119's manifest portion) and issue 74's
  read-only update plan (TSK-247) are in this release by the operator's
  decision of 2026-10-28, and the release waits for them (Version
  relabel amendment below). They are not out of scope. Their tasks are
  TSK-1085, TSK-1086, TSK-1087, TSK-247 and TSK-1088 (Baseline
  replacement amendment, 2026-10-30).
- Related work that stays standalone: TSK-269 on `task/TSK-269-codeql-findings-are-triaged-and-the-design-kit-e`.
  TSK-270 (PR 142) is complete: it merged into `main` on 2026-10-12 and
  reached this line with the merge of `main` through PR 184.
- Closing the issues: each closes when this epic's pull request reaches
  `main`, not when a task lands on the integration line.
- Publishing 4.0.0: tagging and publication stay with the operator under
  `docs/releasing.md`.
<!-- The test integrity amendment of 2026-10-17 follows. -->

## Test integrity amendment (2026-10-17)

Amended 2026-10-17 at the operator's request to make sure every CodeFlow
capability is genuinely tested, with no faking or sabotage. Sources: two
independent read-only audits of that day at line head
`eaf2ed04e932186cdd26dc4a7e5740cc553acee3`, one by Codex and one by Claude
Fable 5.1. Both found no faking or sabotage, and both found real gaps;
each task record copies the findings it relies on. This amendment adds
TSK-361 to TSK-369 and criteria AC-59 to AC-68. Its criteria numbers start
at 59 so they never collide with PR 224 (AC-13, AC-31 to AC-47) or PR 226
(AC-48 to AC-58), and its edits sit on their own lines so the three
amendments can land in any order. Revised the same day after the Codex
review of PR 228 at `dc16feeef` (seven majors and three minors, all
addressed).

| Task | Outcome | Surface | Builder | Security review |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TSK-361 | A test reports skipped or incomplete when its scenario did not run, and macOS-only tests have a lane or recorded run | this repository's tests (run record count if needed) | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | when it adds CI lanes or changes verdict semantics |
| TSK-362 | Capability promises resolve to tests, native records or declared gaps | adopter-facing engine (`validate --docs`) and this repository's registry | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-363 | Policy keys with no reader are unsupported, not documented controls | adopter-facing engine, scaffold and docs | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-364 | A substituted hook binary is refused at every entry point | adopter-facing scaffold (shims) and engine | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-365 | The CI pin journey drives the installed candidate binary | this repository's tests | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-366 | Expectation regeneration never counts as verification | this repository's tests and gate; portal starter script | Codex GPT-6.1 Sol, high | on the gate verdict and the portal recovery path |
| TSK-367 | A bounded mutation campaign over the enforcement code | this repository's tests | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-368 | Native workflow capabilities qualified with current evidence | evidence and registry | Codex GPT-6.1 Sol, high | no |
| TSK-369 | Each landing candidate's full gate records its own coverage | this repository's gate config and evidence | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |

Every reviewer is Claude Fable 5.1 at high, one independent whole-unit
pass, plus `cf-security-reviewer` where the table says so.

```text
TSK-361 --+
TSK-362 --+
TSK-363 --+--> TSK-367 (mutation campaign)
TSK-364 --+
TSK-365 --+
TSK-324 (EPC-023) --> TSK-364
TSK-362 -----> TSK-368 (native qualification)
TSK-331 -----> TSK-369 (exact-candidate coverage)
TSK-366 (root)
```

Folded into existing owners rather than duplicated:

- The in-session refusal of `CODEFLOW_HOOK_BINARY` and of a `PATH` that
  changes the `codeflow` resolution is TSK-324's AC-5 (EPC-023, on this
  line). TSK-364 depends on TSK-324, a real input (the refusal and the
  recorded binary digest); this is the epic's first edge to an EPC-023
  task, and the earlier statement that no task here depends on EPC-023 no
  longer holds for TSK-364.
- PR 224's meaning-taken pack qualification and Codex startup task, and
  TSK-194 (EPC-018, seat qualification), keep their scope. TSK-368 does not
  wait for them; it reuses their evidence only for the portions it
  exercised.
- PR 224's restored test-time task keeps the guard fuzz tests and the
  `rust-coverage` time limit; TSK-331 keeps the machine lock and budget;
  PR 226's fixture and selection tasks keep their scope. TSK-369 coordinates
  with all of them and changes a timeout only with a reason and the
  operator's approval.
- TSK-351 (hosted repair) keeps its fixture repairs; TSK-361 starts after
  it lands.
- TSK-302 keeps `security/interpreter.rs`; TSK-367 only adds tests there,
  one writer at a time.
- The macOS-only refusal tests are owned by TSK-361 AC-6 (a lane or a
  recorded local run per test). PR 224's native platform task covers a
  Firefox presentation run and is not their owner.

Settled here, where the audits differed: `deletion.rs:8239` asserts
`contains("")` on a set of parsed values, which is a real membership check;
the reading of it as vacuous is a false positive. TSK-361 strengthens it to
the exact expected set and records that disposition. CAP-008's stubbed
GitHub boundary is disclosed by TSK-362, which edits every registry entry,
rather than by TSK-368.

Decided by the primary on 2026-10-17, inside the secret floor that is
never relaxed: an impostor hook binary is refused, never admitted with
reduced assurance; TSK-364's ADR settles only how identity is established
and the trusted route for a development binary.

Operator decisions this amendment leaves open:

- Whether mutation testing becomes a standing gate, and on which paths, is
  decided after TSK-367 reports its measured cost and findings.
- Installing a mutation tool (TSK-367) follows the normal tool-adoption
  decision.
- Implementing `security.script_bypass` or `security.sandbox_retry`
  remains a separate future decision; TSK-363 takes the primary's default
  of removing the claimed behaviour.
- Any gate timeout change in TSK-369 needs the operator's approval.

Shared files, one writer at a time and no scheduling-only edges:

- `crates/codeflow-cli/tests/hooks_cli.rs`: TSK-361, TSK-364 and TSK-367.
- `assets/base/git-hooks/*`: TSK-364 and TSK-324.
- `crates/codeflow-core/src/hooks/policy.rs`, `policy_schema.rs` and
  `assets/base/policy.json`: TSK-363 with TSK-276, TSK-292 and TSK-330.
- `docs/capabilities.md` and `crates/codeflow-cli/tests/journey_gate.toml`:
  TSK-362, then TSK-368.
- `crates/codeflow-cli/tests/ci_pin.rs`: TSK-365 with TSK-309 and TSK-333.
- `.codeflow/test-config.json`: TSK-361 and TSK-369 with TSK-331 and the
  gate tasks of PR 224 and PR 226.

## Deferred work

- Issues #203, #200 and #199 were deferred here on 2026-10-17. The operator's
  release decision of 2026-10-18 brings them into 3.2.0 as TSK-384, TSK-383
  and TSK-382; each task carries the constraints the deferral named.
- Superseded on 2026-10-28: the baseline replacement is now in this release (Version relabel amendment below), and the rest of this bullet is its history; its tasks are TSK-1085 to TSK-1087, and TSK-258 is cancelled as superseded by TSK-1087 (2026-10-30). [Manifest without digests, TSK-258/PR 125](https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/pull/125): defer to the approved 4.0.0 baseline replacement, which rewrites the manifest. A separate older schema-2 migration would introduce an intervening format. Revisit when the replacement manifest and managed-drift contract are specified; reconcile TSK-247 against that design and retain PR 125 as evidence. This retains [issue 119](https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/issues/119) as the tracked owner. The old release stamp and migration stay outside 3.2.0. The repeated update conflict from [issue 74](https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/issues/74)'s comment of 2026-10-13 is fixed in 3.2.0 by TSK-405; the baseline replacement carries TSK-405's regression tests forward.

## Affected surfaces and interfaces

- CAP-003 git policy gates: `codeflow ci` range resolution, classification
  and acceptance (`crates/codeflow-cli/src/cmd/ci.rs`, `cmd/ci/`,
  `crates/codeflow-core/src/workgraph/`), git-guard and exec-guard
  (`crates/codeflow-core/src/hooks/`, `crates/codeflow-core/src/security/`),
  the policy schema and `docs/policy-reference.md`, the release preflight
  (`scripts/release.py`, `crates/codeflow-core/src/release_local.rs`),
  CodeFlow's own pushes and their credential, and the managed CI workflow
  (`assets/base/ci/codeflow-ci.yml`).
- CAP-005 integrate: the merge mode (`crates/codeflow-core/src/integrate.rs`).
- CAP-009 and CAP-010: the cf-consult and cf-delegate brief path and
  `cf-herdr` delivery (`assets/base/agents/skills/`,
  `assets/base/claude/skills/`, mirrored into `.claude/` and `.agents/`).
- CAP-013: the `autonomy-with-judgment` pack run, recorded under
  `docs/verification/`.
- CAP-002 scaffold update: `codeflow update` refuses to lower the pin
  (TSK-309, `crates/codeflow-core/src/scaffold/update.rs`), and pins are
  compared and written with one parser (TSK-333,
  `crates/codeflow-core/src/scaffold/version.rs`, `release_pin.rs`,
  `crates/codeflow-core/src/doctor/ci_pin.rs`).
- CAP-003 hooks: the settings read while a settings file is unmerged
  (TSK-330, `crates/codeflow-core/src/hooks/policy.rs`,
  `hooks/landed_policy.rs`).
- CAP-004 test gate: essential runs take the machine gate lock (TSK-331,
  `crates/codeflow-cli/src/cmd/test.rs`,
  `crates/codeflow-core/src/testing/gate_guard.rs`).
- CAP-015 documentation portal: the workflow lease checks its owner
  (TSK-332, `docs-portal/scripts/publication.mjs` and its starter copy
  under `assets/docs-portal/starter/`).
- CAP-004 test gate: the pre-push push set fails closed on a tree it
  cannot check (TSK-304, `crates/codeflow-cli/src/cmd/push_set.rs`).
- Repository-owned automation, never installed in adopter projects: the
  release-integration workflow and its runbook (TSK-310,
  `.github/workflows/codeflow-release-integration.yml`, `docs/releasing.md`).
- Adopters: guards may refuse writes and commands they allowed before
  (TSK-283, TSK-284, TSK-285); new policy keys (TSK-276, TSK-292); a new
  project push setting (TSK-291); a new managed CI hook (TSK-294); a
  pre-push that refuses an unchecked tree at `block` (TSK-304); a
  destination failure reported under its own rule (TSK-305); `update`
  refusing an older binary (TSK-309); hooks that keep working through a
  settings conflict (TSK-330); an essential run that refuses beside
  another gate (TSK-331); portal builds that recover from a killed run at
  once (TSK-332); `update --pin` refusing a pin the parser rejects
  (TSK-333). Each task names its own homes in
  `## Deliverables`; release notes go in `changelog.d/<task id>.md`
  (ADR-0082).

- Credential inventory and native presets: `crates/codeflow-core/src/security/actions.json`, `assets/base/claude/`, `assets/base/codex/`, `assets/base/grok/` and installed mirrors.
- Shell secret matcher and hosted policy authority: `crates/codeflow-core/src/security/`, `assets/base/ci/codeflow-policy.yml` and installed workflows.
- Codex launch and preflight instructions: mirrored orchestration and customization skills under `assets/base/agents/skills/` and `assets/base/claude/skills/`.
- First adoption, imported completion, repeated closeout and stacked pins: `crates/codeflow-core/src/workgraph/` and CLI push/CI callers.
- YAML readers and writers: `crates/codeflow-core/src/`, `crates/codeflow-cli/src/`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` and `osv-scanner.toml`.
- Qualification and repository platform automation: `evals/qualification/`, `docs/verification/`, `.github/workflows/codeflow-ci.yml`, presentation and portal journeys.

Landing the 2026-10-16 amendment beside TSK-309 and TSK-329: both tasks
were completed on their own branches (PRs 207 and 205), and this amendment
changes their assignment lines. Prefer landing PRs 205 and 207 before the
amendment; a later clean planning amendment keeps their completion
bindings. If the amendment lands first, either land the already reviewed
task heads directly in clean merges into the line, or, if a task branch is
refreshed by merging the amended line, get a new whole-unit review and
completion binding at the refreshed head: the assignment lines then differ
from the reviewed record outside status and Closeout, and the binding check
refuses it. Never copy the old acceptance block onto a refreshed head or
treat the refresh as safe without that review. In any merge, keep both the
closeouts and the reassignment lines.

## Gate efficiency amendment (2026-10-17)

Amended 2026-10-17 at the operator's request to make the verification
gates fast from first principles: remove runs that add no value, keep real
redundancy for security at the right levels, and make sure the right checks
run locally and in hosted CI. Sources: the Codex peer discovery on gate
speed and the hosted-red diagnosis, both of 2026-10-17 at line head
`eaf2ed04e932186cdd26dc4a7e5740cc553acee3`. Each task record copies the
figures it relies on. This amendment adds TSK-352 to TSK-360 and TSK-370,
amends TSK-331 and adds criteria AC-48 to AC-58. Its criteria numbers
start at 48 so that they never collide with the planning amendment in PR
224 (AC-13 and AC-31 to AC-47); its edits sit in their own lines so either
amendment can land first. Revised the same day after the Codex review of
PR 226 at `fb640cef5` (five majors and five minors, all addressed). A confirmation review at `dd6792c17` found two more majors (raw
environment values in run records, and the pipeline's update ownership),
both addressed. The build-speed research of the same day then replaced the
sccache pilot in TSK-357 with build profile and test layout work (builder
changed to Codex GPT-6 Astra) and gave clippy its own target directory in
TSK-331 AC-9.

Decisions of 2026-10-17 that this plan carries:

- Builders no longer run routine whole essential or full gates. Their
  evidence is targeted tests plus the quick gate, as the managed block of
  `AGENTS.md` already says; targeted heavy-mode checks and measurement
  trials a task authorizes stay allowed; one full gate runs on each landing
  candidate. TSK-360 writes this into the project section and TSK-370 into
  the shipped pipeline default for fresh installs.
- These assurance layers stay, each for a distinct failure it catches:
  independent review of each unit; one full local gate per landing
  candidate; the hosted Linux and Windows gates; the hosted secret scan and
  its gitleaks allowlist canary; the osv-scanner security review; CodeQL;
  and the live pre-push protections.
- The process gap closes: hosted Linux and Windows gates give evidence
  before a pull request lands on an integration line (TSK-358, on ready
  pull requests only), and ordinary landings wait for green hosted verdicts
  on the line's current tip, with a recovery exception for the repair
  (TSK-360).

| Task | Outcome | Surface | Builder | Security review |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TSK-352 | Selection skips only what proven evidence covers, against the task's line | adopter-facing engine, config schema and pre-push hook | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-353 | Repository targets declare the inputs they read and their input class | this repository's gate config | Codex GPT-6.1 Sol, high | yes, for security suites only |
| TSK-354 | Essential Rust runs under nextest with doctests, same membership | this repository's gate config | Codex GPT-6.1 Sol, high | no |
| TSK-355 | Completed target executions are reused; push protections stay live | adopter-facing engine and pre-push hook | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-356 | Repeated fixtures run once; exhaustive matrices go to the full gate | this repository's tests and gate config | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-331 (amended) | One heavy gate at a time, heavy quick work admitted by the same lock, one worker budget inside a gate | adopter-facing engine (lock, admission) and this repository's config (budget) | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high (reassigned) | no |
| TSK-357 | Rust build profiles cut disk and runtime without new tools; compiler cache not adopted now | this repository's build profiles and tests | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | no |
| TSK-358 | Hosted Linux and Windows gates on ready pull requests into lines | this repository's CI | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-359 | A push reuses only the hosted verdict that tested its exact merge | this repository's CI | Codex GPT-6 Astra, high | yes |
| TSK-360 | Contract states builder evidence and the hosted landing rule | this repository's contract | Codex GPT-6.1 Sol, high | yes, plus editorial review |
| TSK-370 | Fresh installs get a pipeline that verifies with targeted checks and quick; existing copies get an edit notice and a doctor finding | adopter-facing scaffold and engine | Codex GPT-6.1 Sol, high | no |

Every reviewer is Claude Fable 5.1 at high, one independent whole-unit
pass, plus `cf-security-reviewer` where the table says so. Seats are
confirmed at dispatch.

Where the time goes, so the ranking follows value. Under the builder
decision, the essential run that the discovery measured (49 to 54 target
minutes, about 29 to 31 of them `rust-workspace`) no longer runs per task.
The remaining recurring costs are the pre-push quick run on every push (1
to 3 target minutes, once 10.5 with build waits; about 91 seconds of
workspace clippy on this amendment's records-only pushes), the full gate on
each landing candidate (the coverage serial group alone has a floor of
about 12.3 minutes), and the hosted runs (one failed-run sample of
2026-10-17: 157.25 summed job minutes, 30m42s from start to last update,
a fan-out of 19 jobs). Value ranking: TSK-356 (the full gate's serial path),
TSK-352 with TSK-355 (quick and repeat runs) and TSK-359 (a duplicate
hosted run per landing) carry most of the saving; TSK-354 and TSK-353
matter when an essential or subset run is still made, and to adopters
through TSK-352.

2. TSK-354, TSK-352 and the amended TSK-331 (roots).

   TSK-351; TSK-357 after TSK-354 and TSK-331.
4. TSK-358 after TSK-351; TSK-359 after TSK-358, when its runs show a pair.

Operator decisions, as of 2026-10-17:

- Decided by the operator: hosted Linux and Windows gates on ready pull
  requests into integration lines and the hosted-green landing rule (from
  the request to make sure the right checks run locally and in CI);
  extending hosted verdict reuse to integration lines; changing the shipped
  adopter pipeline default to targeted checks plus quick per change with
  one full gate per landing, for fresh installs only, with the pipeline
  staying user-owned: existing copies are never rewritten, the release
  note and update output give the one-line edit, and `codeflow doctor`
  reports the old default (TSK-370); and a GitHub ruleset requiring the hosted
  `codeflow gates` and `windows` checks on `integration/*`, applied once
  TSK-351 makes the line green, with the primary preparing it and showing
  its exact content before applying it (TSK-358 AC-6). Superseded in part
  on 2026-10-18: the operator applies the ruleset after TSK-358 lands,
  with `strict_required_status_checks_policy: false` (Release scope
  amendment, settled decisions).
- Replaced by the primary on 2026-10-17: the operator approved an sccache
  pilot only if it would be effective; the build-speed research of that day
  showed it is not the right first lever (the essential Rust step is 79 to
  98 percent serial test execution, warm compiles take 35 to 133 seconds,
  and sccache's Rust key includes the working directory). TSK-357 now cuts
  build profiles and test layout instead, and records a revisit event for a
  cache. Any XProtect exemption it measures stays the operator's decision.
- Still pending: none in this amendment.

Recorded for the later 4.0.0 planning, not as a task here: the operator
decided on 2026-10-17 that the 4.0.0 baseline work keeps every published
release's bases in the binary.

Not in this amendment: nextest or caching defaults for adopters, and any
change to the secret scan, canary, security review or CodeQL.

## Release scope amendment (2026-10-18)

The operator approved the release plan on 2026-10-18: 3.2.0, then 4.0.0
(the baseline replacement only), then 4.1.0. The controlling rule: every
security gap, and every defect that forces an adopter workaround or blocks
work, belongs in 3.2.0 whatever its original severity. Feature
generalizations whose manual route works, qualification and evaluation,
the test-integrity audit and the 4.0.0 baseline work go later. This
amendment applies that rule to this epic and to EPC-023, which becomes the
4.1.0 epic. It was revised the same day after the Codex review of PR 249
(`target/review-PR249-codex-01.md` in the planning worktree), which found
security repairs and adopter workarounds left in 4.1.0 or unassigned; the
revision below is the one that lands.

```text
                            EPC-022 (3.2.0)          EPC-023 (4.1.0)
moved in from EPC-023       TSK-322..325
moved out to EPC-023                                 288 289 303 344 346
                                                     347 348 361 362 365..369
new, 3.2.0                  TSK-378..389, 391..393,
                            395, 397..405
new, 4.1.0                                           TSK-390, TSK-394
cancelled                                            TSK-396 (covered by TSK-379)
```

Operator decisions this amendment left open were settled on 2026-10-18
under the operator's direction of that day to decide from research and
ask only for approvals the operator must give (workspace
`RELEASE-PLAN.md`, "Settled under it the same day"):

- The same-line symlink residual is refused, not accepted. A link created
  earlier on the same command line is in the shell grammar the guards
  model (operator decision of 2026-10-15 on TSK-302's scope), so a line
  where a link-creating command runs before a writer or interpreter
  refuses. TSK-407 does this (amendment below).
- F3: no new review rule for pull requests that change
  `.github/workflows/**`. The decision holds on the primary's review of
  every pull request and on TSK-336, which makes the secret scan trusted
  against a head that disables it; a required-review ruleset would also
  block the sole account's own pull requests. Corrected fact, checked at
  `236e9d08b` with that tree's built hooks: until TSK-325 lands, the
  in-session guards refuse writes to `.github/workflows/codeflow-ci.yml`
  only (`git_guard.rs:3706`); a native Write, a shell redirect, a rename or
  an interpreter write to any other workflow file passes. TSK-325, widened
  by this amendment to every file under `.github/workflows/`, closes the
  rest in 3.2.0.
- The integration ruleset keeps `strict_required_status_checks_policy:
  false`. Each landing gates the exact candidate and the line's push run
  checks the merged tree, so strict mode would rerun hosted CI on every
  line move without added assurance. The ruleset is TSK-358's AC-6; the
  operator applies it after TSK-358 lands.
- The GitHub milestone edits named above were approved by the operator
  and applied on 2026-10-18 after PR 249 landed.

resolver for link names and targets. It also widens TSK-325 (AC-71) from the policy workflow to every
file under `.github/workflows/`, the gap found while recording F3, and

| TSK-407 | A link created earlier on a command line is refused like an existing link | PR 239 security review, medium | Codex GPT-6 Astra | yes, with Grok 4.7 attacker lens |

## Recursive copy, hard link and parallel amendment (2026-10-20)

This amendment gives an owner to the 3.2.0 security gaps the reviews of
PR 260 (TSK-379) and PR 264 (TSK-380) left without one, under the
operator's rule of 2026-10-18 that every security gap belongs in 3.2.0,
and amends SPC-013 R-50 to state the amendment-note boundary that TSK-382
(PR 266) implements. It adds TSK-408 and the criterion AC-99, widens
TSK-407 (AC-97) with hard links and TSK-378 (AC-74) with GNU `parallel`
replacement strings, and widens the AC-98 journey to TSK-408. Graph v10
added TSK-408 as a root; graph v11 adds the edges TSK-380 -> TSK-407 and
TSK-380 -> TSK-408, recording that both tasks edit `security/startup.rs`
after PR 264's root walk lands. No other node or edge changes.

| Task | Change | Source | Builder | Security review |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TSK-408 | New: a recursive copy or sync is judged by the enforcement files it creates, replaces or deletes | PR 260 security review F6, high | Codex GPT-6 Astra | yes, with Grok 4.7 attacker lens |
| TSK-407 | Widened: a hard link of an enforcement file, a checkout's git pointer or a startup-class file is a write of that file, on the creating command, on later writes to its name, and when the `rg --pre` root walk or the native walk `holds_enforcement_files` meets it (AC-8) | PR 260 Grok review F-HL, high; PR 264 Grok confirm round 2 F1, major; the startup-class direct operand, this amendment | unchanged | unchanged |
| TSK-378 | Widened: a `parallel` `{= perl =}` replacement string or `--rpl` definition is judged as `perl -e` code in addition to the line's existing checks (AC-6) | PR 264 security review P-2, high | unchanged | unchanged |

The reviewer of TSK-408 is Claude Fable 5.1 at high, one independent
whole-unit pass, plus `cf-security-reviewer` and Grok 4.7 at high as the
attacker lens. Seats are confirmed at dispatch. TSK-407 and TSK-378 keep
their seats; neither has started, so the widening rides here, not in a
task PR.

- SPC-013 R-50 now says a tag is read on the criterion's own text, which
  excludes each amendment note (a dash sub-bullet whose first word is
  `Amended` or `Moved` and whose next word is a `YYYY-MM-DD` date, with
  its wrapped continuation) and keeps every other sub-bullet and wrapped
  line. The spec's bound consumers and their dispositions are named in
  the note. TSK-382's outcome and AC-1 said a criterion's text ends at
  its first `- Amended ...` or `- Moved ...` sub-bullet, which is wider
  than this boundary; its builder narrows them to the dated dash note in
  PR 266, the task's own PR, since a task may change its own criteria
  there (R-52) and an edit of `TSK-382.md` here would conflict with that
  open PR. AC-78 is reworded to the same boundary.
- Shared files, one writer at a time, no edge: TSK-408 shares
  `git_guard.rs`, `security/unresolved.rs` and `security/deletion.rs`
  with TSK-379 and TSK-342; it is dispatched after PR 260 lands, because
  that PR repairs F5 (the `rsync --remove-sent-files` alias) in the same
  copy judge. TSK-407's hard-link check adds `hooks/edit_guard.rs` to its
  shared files with TSK-379 and TSK-399. `security/startup.rs` is shared
  by TSK-407 (the inode compare inside the `rg --pre` root walk) and
  TSK-408 (`ExecWriter::copies`), one writer at a time, both after PR 264
  lands; the edges TSK-380 -> TSK-407 and TSK-380 -> TSK-408 record that
  order. `security/deletion.rs` is shared by TSK-378 (`--plus` and
  `--rpl` modelled in `input_options_unproven` and `judge_parallel`) and
  TSK-408 (`judge_rsync`), one writer at a time, no edge.
- TSK-408 records archive extraction (`tar -x`, `unzip`) as a stated
  residual with the sandbox backstop; a reviewer who finds it reachable
  on the installed candidate returns it to the plan as a finding.
- Found in TSK-380's confirm round (Grok 4.7 round 2 of PR 264 at
  `1e1965723`, F1, major, live) and assigned to TSK-407 by the primary
  on 2026-10-20: a hard link of `~/.zshrc` or of `.claude/settings.json`
  under another name inside a directory that `rg --pre tee` walks passes
  both guards, and the live run empties the original; symbolic links in
  the same place are refused. TSK-407 AC-8 now requires the `rg --pre`
  root walk and the native walk `holds_enforcement_files` to treat an
  entry whose device and inode match a startup-class or enforcement file
  as that file, by identity and never by link count; the content a
  recursive copy or sync moves stays TSK-408's. PR 264 keeps its own
  `--pre` root walk; the inode compare inside that walk is TSK-407's.
  A direct operand that is a hard link of a startup file (`tee notes`
  with `notes` sharing the inode of `~/.zshrc`) is outside F1, which
  judged only the walk, and was not covered by TSK-407's pre-walk
  wording either, so AC-8 adds it as one spelled case under the same
  inode rule, refused by exec-guard. AC-97 names the startup-class file.

This amendment places the 18 open issues that had no task on
2026-10-22, under three operator decisions of that day: the 3.2.0
release rule of 2026-10-18 stands (every security gap and every defect
that forces an adopter workaround or blocks work belongs in 3.2.0);
project-management changes (how epics, tasks, lines and records work)
also go in 3.2.0; the larger feature requests go to one separate,
smaller epic sized by issue 280's guidance; and no scope is cut from
existing 3.2.0 work. It also adds TSK-433 for a defect the primary found
while rehearsing this epic's pull request into `main`, and TSK-434 for
the operator's direction that an integration branch is deletable only
after it is merged, TSK-435 for the operator's approval of an explicit
Outcome on records and Deliverables on epics, and moves TSK-311 and
TSK-312 in from EPC-023 by the operator's decision of the same day.
Graph v12 adds the new nodes and edges; the criteria AC-100 to AC-124
are new, and
AC-28, AC-34 and AC-37 are widened in place with dated notes.

```text
                              EPC-022 (3.2.0)        EPC-024 (next epic)
new tasks                     TSK-411..421, 433..437 TSK-424..432, 438
                              TSK-441, 443..446
moved out to EPC-025          TSK-422, TSK-423       (EPC-025: 422, 423, 439, 440)
moved in from EPC-023         TSK-311, TSK-312
widened in place              TSK-331, 337, 340
issues placed                 250 251 253 254 257    274 275 279 252 234
                              258 262 273 276 277    286 (items 2, 3)
                              278 280 272 287
                              286 (item 1)
```

Seats: each new task's builder is a Claude worker, a native subagent of
the primary's session, Sonnet 5.5 at high for routine work and Opus 5.5
at high for guard, hook and acceptance-binding work (TSK-418, TSK-421
and TSK-433 are Opus for the reason each record states). The reviewer
of each is Codex GPT-6 Astra at high in an interactive Herdr seat, one
independent whole-unit pass, so the review stays with the lineage that
does not build it; the primary routes Grok 4.7 at high as the
cross-family reviewer when both lineages authored a unit, and
`cf-security-reviewer` plus the Grok attacker lens where the table says.
Seats are confirmed at dispatch. The widened tasks keep their seats;
none has started, so the widening rides here.

Placement, and where it differs from the triage list the primary gave:

- #276 widens TSK-331 rather than adding a task: both want one lock
  that every gate mode respects. TSK-331 already makes heavy quick
  targets and the pre-push gate wait or refuse (its AC-2, amended
  2026-10-17); what it lacked is the issue's three asks, which AC-12 and
  AC-13 add: a project setting for the default class of an undeclared
  quick target (the reporting project's quick suites run 420 to 456 s and
  are undeclared), the wait bound applying to the pre-push run with a
  message naming the holder, and a readable holder record (pid,
  worktree, mode, start). AC-28 is reworded, since it still said quick
  runs take no lock.
- #254 widens TSK-337, whose outcome is already that a prescribed Codex
  launch has its intended builder permissions: the missing grants are two
  lines in the same `assets/base/codex/config.toml` profile, and the
  doctor report is the Claude preset's existing check extended.
- #278 is split: its body (an in-branch waiver after a reopen) widens
  TSK-340, which owns the reopened completion sequence (#217); its
  comment (completion judged against a stale local `main`) joins #262 in
  TSK-416, the same local-first lookup in `target_reference_names`.
- #262 is a new task, not a widening of TSK-341 (chains) or TSK-398
  (`codeflow ci` anchors, complete): the lookup it fixes is in
  `work_start.rs` and feeds `work claim` and `task status`.
- #258 and #273 are two tasks, both in `git_guard.rs`'s readers: #258 is
  the sed judge and the unresolved-sed check (TSK-290's residual), #273
  the segmenter's quoting across newlines and braces; one writer at a
  time, and both after TSK-406's resolver lands.
- #280 is three tasks by outcome: guidance (TSK-419), the drift view
  (TSK-420) and the two line commands (TSK-421); the per-stream view it asks
  for in the program report is `codeflow coord status` (TSK-440); the
  record-only report defers it (TSK-311's note).
- #272 is its own small epic, EPC-025 (the coordination ledger: follow-ups and lanes),
  as the operator directed on 2026-10-22: four tasks (TSK-422 the
  ledger and `follow` command, TSK-439 lanes and ids, TSK-440 the hooks
  on all three harnesses, TSK-423 the rules and skills) and ADR-0086,
  proposed in this planning pull request. It targets 3.2.0 as the
  operator placed issue 272 there. Amended 2026-10-23: its tasks land
  on EPC-025's own line,
  `integration/EPC-025-the-coordination-ledger-follow-ups-and-lanes`,
  cut from this line, because the epic-line binding
  (`crates/codeflow-core/src/workgraph/work_start.rs:880-917`) requires
  a task's epic to equal the line's epic, so the first EPC-025 push was
  refused as "not a bound epic line" and fell back to `main`'s binding
  findings (`.worktrees/tsk-422/target/push-ci-TSK-422.log`); the
  2026-10-22 plan had its tasks landing here and the Codex review of PR
  285 did not catch it. The closing step: when EPC-025's tasks have
  landed and its criteria are proven on its own candidate, its line
  merges into this one through one reviewed pull request classified as
  this line's merge, gated once as a candidate here, before this epic's
  pull request to `main`, which then describes both epics; the EPC-025
  journey (its AC-5) is proven again on that final candidate. Shared
  files between the two lines (`orient.rs`, the Claude presets,
  `settings_merge.rs`, the managed block, `cf-method`, `cf-herdr`,
  `cf-delegate`, `cf-present` resources) stay one writer at a time
  across lines, and the EPC-025 line merges this line in before each
  of its tasks that edits one. TSK-422 and TSK-423, first
  allocated here, moved with their criteria (AC-111 and AC-112 are
  EPC-025 AC-1 and AC-4); TSK-423 keeps its edge from TSK-387.
  The design was reviewed independently by a Codex seat (sound with
  changes, 22 findings, 14 high) and the records follow the settled
  version 7 of the design page; the ledger is an aid, never a control.
- The operator's cf-present direction of 2026-10-22 is five tasks, one
  outcome each, restructured the same day from a single TSK-441 that
  had grown to carry all five (every criterion and finding moved to
  exactly one task, none dropped): TSK-441 the default surface (today

  not walls of text; anchor and build so the reader never connects the

  TSK-445 before TSK-446. TSK-442, allocated for a separate palette
  task that day, was withdrawn before landing; its number stays
  reserved and is not reused.
- TSK-433 is new, not a widening: TSK-339 judges a completion imported

- TSK-311 and TSK-312 move in from EPC-023 with their criteria (EPC-023

- The later comments on #280 (2026-10-22 17:35, one epic at a time per

  TSK-416 AC-2. #275's revised request of 2026-10-22 13:59 (strict by

- The five feature requests (#274, #275, #279, #252, #234) are EPC-024,
  one epic as the operator directed, with TSK-424 to TSK-432. Its
  Summary states one value line in three parts and the split issue 280's

## Process cuts amendment (2026-10-23)

Two independent process audits of the 3.2.0 line (Claude and Codex, read at
`c9993bcf3`) found the cost in checks that rerun on unchanged work, reviews
that reread the whole unit, facts recorded in several places and prose rules
held at block. On 2026-10-23 the operator approved both decisions of the
merged proposal (the dependency audit becomes severity-aware; the security
review becomes one cross-lineage whole-unit pass with the security lens) and
every no-approval change in it. The git hooks, both guards, the secret scan,
the trusted `codeflow ci` job, the full gate on the landing candidate and the
human merge are unchanged. Graph v13 adds TSK-450 and TSK-451 as roots and TSK-449
and TSK-452 behind TSK-331 (its admission lock), with no edges among the four; the criteria AC-125 to AC-128 are new.

| Task | Outcome | Builder |
|---|---|---|
| TSK-449 | Push and CI gates stop rerunning unchanged work | Claude Opus 5.5 |
| TSK-450 | Style rules warn, the dependency audit weighs severity, unread keys go | Claude Opus 5.5 |
| TSK-451 | One record per completed fact | Claude Opus 5.5 |
| TSK-452 | Review cadence and guidance state each rule once, and planned work moves at speed | Claude Opus 5.5 |

Notes:

- Dispatch contract. Heavy work (the full gate, essential, heavy quick
  targets, direct heavy Cargo commands) takes TSK-331's resource admission;
  cheap targeted checks run freely. TSK-449 and TSK-452 depend on TSK-331
  (the pre-push targets leave after its admission lands; the guidance names
  the lock). TSK-353 owns every input declaration and conditional skip
  (TSK-449 declares none). TSK-450 owns the dependency-audit step in both
  `codeflow-ci.yml` and the shipped template; TSK-449 owns the rest of the
  workflow and template. TSK-451 owns the Testing and release-impact hunks of
  `ci/pr_body.rs`; TSK-450 the Summary and characters hunks; the pre-push
  release-impact call in `push_set.rs` stays with TSK-449 and does not change.
  TSK-449 owns the one test-config tier edit and the `test_gate_on_push`
  entry; TSK-450 owns every other policy line. The managed baseline is
  resynced after merging the line in.
- Other open tasks edit the same files and keep one writer at a time:
  `push_set.rs` and `test-config.json` (TSK-327, TSK-338, TSK-341, TSK-393,
  TSK-331 and others), `codeflow-ci.yml` (TSK-294, TSK-303, TSK-412 and
  others), `policy_schema.rs` and `policy-reference.md` (many), `ci/acceptance`
  (TSK-433, TSK-436), `amendment.rs` (TSK-414, TSK-418), and the managed
  guidance (TSK-419, TSK-423, TSK-435, TSK-441, TSK-387). No edges are added.
- The operator set the throughput expectation in TSK-452 (each build lane
  delivers at least one planned task to ready-for-review per hour, preferably
  two) on 2026-10-23, which satisfies "Only the operator adds process".
- Tracked once, not planned here: automating the advisory triage item and
  the update PR, a session qualification record, and worktree claims.
  Revisit when the operator asks or at the first amendment after these four
  tasks land.

## EPC-025 line merge amendment (2026-10-24)

The operator chose on 2026-10-24 to merge the EPC-025 line into this line
now, with TSK-422 landed there (PR 311), so the 3.2.0 pull request to
`main` can open. EPC-025 planned that merge as one pull request with its
line as the head, but `codeflow ci` lands an `integration/` head only on
the default branch (`work_start.rs:733-741`), and the hosted check judges
against this line, so it refuses that head here. TSK-1000 carries the
merge as an ordinary task of this epic: its branch merges the EPC-025
line, TSK-422's completed record is imported unchanged and bound at its
home, and the task's own work is the conflict resolution and the
subcommand count. Graph v14 adds TSK-1000 as a root with no edges: it
carries TSK-422's work rather than building on it, and TSK-422 is not
complete on this line until the merge lands. The criterion AC-129 is new.

| Task | Outcome | Builder |
|---|---|---|
| TSK-1000 | The EPC-025 line joins the 3.2.0 line | Claude Opus 5.5 |

Notes:

- The same amendment rewords TSK-422's AC-1 here to the text its own pull
  request settled on the EPC-025 line (8f5b4c9bb, the operator-role
  residual), so the record this line carries matches the record it will
  import; a reopened task keeps its criteria as the target has them, and
  `codeflow ci` names this epic's batched amendment as the place to change
  them.
- TSK-1000 is numbered after a local probe record held TSK-999 when the id
  was reserved; the gap is not damage (`codeflow ids check`).
- EPC-025's other tasks (TSK-423, TSK-439, TSK-440) stay on its line; each
  later merge of that line into this one goes through a task pull request
  of the same kind.

## Untracked issues amendment (2026-10-26)

Fourteen open issues had no task or epic record that cited them on
2026-10-26: #288, #296, #299 to #304, #320, #322, #332, #334, #341 and
#342. This amendment places each one under the release rule of
2026-10-18 (every security gap and every defect that forces an adopter
workaround or blocks work belongs in 3.2.0; feature generalizations
whose manual route works go later), folding into open work where an
open task's outcome already covers the issue. Six fold into six todo
tasks of this epic; #299's defect half becomes TSK-1014 here; and eight
issues become nine new tasks in EPC-023 (4.1.0) and EPC-024 (4.2.0).
Graph v15 adds TSK-1014 as a root, and the criterion AC-130 is new.

| Issue | Disposition | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [#342](https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/issues/342) | TSK-417 AC-7; comment: TSK-415 AC-7 | the body's quoted `>` is the quoting reader TSK-417 owns and passes at `0f08dd7f8` (regression test plus the unparseable-line message); the comment's `|`-delimited `sed -i` still fails there and is the sed reader's case |
| [#341](https://github.com/sathyassn/codeflow/issues/341) | TSK-433 AC-7 | a line range silently judged as frozen, and "the completion" naming the range head: the line-into-`main` judgment TSK-433 owns |

#354 to #358. Each forces an adopter workaround or blocks work, so under
the release rule of 2026-10-18 all belong in 3.2.0. Three issue parts
fold into open tasks of this epic whose outcome already covers them;
the rest become seven new tasks, TSK-1023 to TSK-1029. Graph v16 adds
TSK-1023, TSK-1025 and TSK-1028 as roots and the edges TSK-397 ->
TSK-1024, TSK-359 -> TSK-1026, TSK-384 -> TSK-1026, TSK-437 -> TSK-1027
and TSK-1028 -> TSK-1029; no existing edge changes. AC-131 to AC-137 are
new.

content, tests and coverage, typing, declaring values once and
frameworks, and decided that each part small on its own (instruction,
review-question or documentation changes) ships in 3.2.0, while gates
and default changes that need TSK-424's baseline and exemption register
stay in EPC-024 (4.2.0). The repository-content instruction fix (issue
#279) was placed in 3.2.0 the same day. Two defects found the same day
join them. Graph v17 adds TSK-1030 to TSK-1035 as roots and the edge
TSK-340 -> TSK-1036; no existing edge changes. AC-138 to AC-144 are new.

## Away session amendment (2026-10-28)

The operator asked on 2026-10-28 for a standard way to leave a session
running while away: work keeps going without stalling, is done by the
procedures and rules, is tracked, and ends in one consolidated report
with action items. The same day the operator widened it to any purpose
and any time (no time-of-day framing, adapting to how the user engages)
and set the base rule for new work. No issue or record covered it. It
joins this epic under the standing direction that features go in the
current release, as three tasks with AC-149 to AC-151 (AC-148 is
TSK-1044's, from the Gate evidence amendment below). Graph v20 (after
that amendment's v19) adds TSK-1047 as a root and the edges TSK-439 -> TSK-1045, TSK-1045 -> TSK-1046, TSK-1047
-> TSK-1046 and TSK-1038 -> TSK-1046; no other edge changes. TSK-439 is
an EPC-025 task on the EPC-025 line. TSK-1045 is dispatched only after
TSK-439 has been carried into this line by merging the EPC-025 line
into it (as on 2026-10-24), and starts from this line's tip; there is
no early start on the EPC-025 line. EPC-023's TSK-1037 is amended to
share the interval key and route reference.

| Item | Task | Placement and reason |
|---|---|---|
| Durable activation, decision index, owed actions and report (`codeflow away`) | TSK-1045 | the state that survives compaction, restarts and usage limits; owed actions reuse the ledger's follow-ups with a new action class |
| The away skill (`/cf-away`, working name) and its wiring | TSK-1046 | the procedure over `autonomy.md`; schedules ticks with each harness's own route, since CodeFlow schedules nothing |
| New work starts from the live tip of the branch that holds what it needs, or a reviewed pin | TSK-1047 | a defect: `work claim` starts from the live target tip, but `work start` and `codeflow ci` do not check the base, and the cross-line route is unproven |

Notes:

- Each new task starts with a broad investigation criterion, listed
  first, by the operator's rule of 2026-10-27.
- Design choice, reversible: a skill plus a small command, not a mode of
  TSK-1037's sweep (a read-only checklist with no record, in 4.1.0) and
  not the pipeline preset (single-vendor batch work). TSK-1037's sweep
  becomes the tick's state read when it lands.
- The interval default is 30 minutes, the operator's sweep decision of
  2026-10-27, held in one key (`[sweep] interval_minutes`) that TSK-1045
  defines and TSK-1037 reads.
- TSK-1047's gaps are from code reading on `654935d52`, not reproduced;
  its AC-1 reproduces them.
- Both lineages discovered independently; Codex's discovery (GPT-6
  Astra) reached the same shape and is reconciled in TSK-1046. It placed
  the work in 4.1.0 after TSK-1037, TSK-1041 and TSK-1042; the plan
  keeps 3.2.0 by the operator's standing direction, as recorded in the
  coordinator's operator decisions, and shapes TSK-1045 to wait on no
  4.1.0 task. The operator confirms the placement when back, and decides
  whether to pull those three into 3.2.0. TSK-1038, already in 3.2.0,
  becomes a predecessor of TSK-1046.
- Codex's review of this plan (round 1) restored TSK-439 as a
  predecessor of TSK-1045 (a caller with no claimed role only reads the
  ledger), settled one base rule in TSK-1047 that keeps reviewed pins
  and fails closed when the remote cannot be checked, made engagement
  change only how questions are delivered, and added ownership,
  concurrency, resource-limit, continuation-route and purpose criteria.
  Its second round kept TSK-1047's claimed base in Git or claim
  metadata, never the ledger (no gate reads it), set what each limit
  type stops, and removed TSK-1045's early start on the EPC-025 line.
- Each task's review carries the security checklist: TSK-1045 renders
  agent and peer text into a report a person acts on, TSK-1046 governs
  unwatched sessions, TSK-1047 changes admission checks.

## Gate evidence amendment (2026-10-28)

The Codex security review of TSK-337 (PR 375) on 2026-10-27 found two
defects in the durable gate evidence under `~/.codeflow/gate-runs`,
which any process running as the user can write, agent sessions
included: a full gate's evidence copy follows planted links (CWE-59,
`testing/gate.rs:513`, `:629`), and local selection credit trusts
run records anyone can write (CWE-345, `testing/delivery.rs:330`,
`:557`, `:617`). Both reach Claude sessions today through the shipped
presets' `gate-runs` write grant. They are filed as TSK-1044 with
AC-148, in 3.2.0 under the operator's secure-by-default rule of
2026-10-24.

Graph v19 adds TSK-337 -> TSK-1044 and TSK-1044 -> TSK-355,
TSK-1044 -> TSK-353 and TSK-1044 -> TSK-1039; TSK-1039 is no longer a
root.

Notes:

- TSK-337 (PR 375) drops the `gate-runs` grant from the Codex
  `cf-builder` profile and amends its own AC-5 in that PR; TSK-1044
  builds on that landed decision and decides the Claude presets.
- TSK-355 (result reuse) reads the same records, so it now depends on
  TSK-1044 and gains AC-9, an investigation listed first that applies
  TSK-1044's trust rule.
- Link-following writes affect every gate that writes evidence,
  default full runs included, on every platform, Windows junctions and
  reparse points included. Only the selection-credit forgery spares
  hosted CI (`--all`), default full runs, integration and Windows; it
  reaches quick, essential and `full --since` runs. This repository
  declares no reusable content target today.
- The Claude preset grant and any protected recorder are the operator's
  decision (a material security boundary); TSK-1044's builder brings the
  options with each full-gate route. Until then: writable reports earn no
  credit, an unresolved grant or recorder authorizes nothing that
  depends on it (TSK-353's skips, TSK-355's reuse, TSK-1039's `--since`
  narrowing), and the containment repairs proceed.
  - Decided 2026-10-30 (TSK-1044 AC-4): the operator dropped the Claude presets' `gate-runs` grant and adopted no local recorder; full gates run outside the sandbox, in the operator's terminal or as an approved unsandboxed run. Row B below applies to TSK-353, TSK-355 and TSK-1039. TSK-1044's record (PR 419) holds the decision and its options.
- Credit rows: TSK-353, TSK-355 and TSK-1039 each carry the same
  two-row table. Row A, an adopted protected recorder: the positive
  narrowing, skip and reuse journeys are required. Row B, no recorder:
  every requested target runs with the reason, no output recommends
  `--since` where it cannot narrow, and the positive journeys are marked
  deferred here. AC-49, AC-51, AC-125 and AC-146 follow the same rows.
- TSK-355 is reconciled with TSK-449: reuse moves from the push to
  explicit gate runs (AC-7, AC-8), and AC-10 covers the no-recorder
  outcome. TSK-353 gains AC-7 (investigation) and its skip and journey
  criteria follow TSK-1044. TSK-1039's `--since` narrowing follows
  TSK-1044's contract. TSK-1014 shares `gate.rs`: a file overlap, not an
  edge.
- The review is cross-lineage: a Claude builder and a Codex reviewer
  carrying the `cf-security-reviewer` checklist.
