## Issue 380 amendment (2026-10-28)

Issue #380 (opened 2026-10-28 05:54Z): the pre-push hook blocks a
task's own criteria change on an integration line with
`work.criteria_frozen`, while `codeflow ci --into <line>` gives a note.
Planning reproduced it on line tip `654935d52` with a scratch CLI
fixture: the block appears only when main and the line hold versions of
the task that neither history contains, and only with the hook's
`--policy-from <main> --commits-from <main>`.

Placement: folded into TSK-350 (3.2.0) as AC-6 (the bounded exception),
AC-8 (the protections that keep their verdicts) and AC-9 (a report of
competing copies at the line landing), with AC-7 as its investigation
listed first. TSK-350's outcome (EPC-022 AC-47) is that
own-task criteria depend on the judged head and explicit authorities,
with one verdict from every entry point; it was widened the same way
for #304 and #354. Checked and not the owner: TSK-306 (complete; a
line's newer version wins over a copy main inherited, not a diverged
one), TSK-295 (complete; completion before main holds the task),
TSK-340 (complete; status changes and repeated closeout), and TSK-1025
(standalone follow-up records). No graph change.

Notes:

- The rule conflicts with AC-16 and SPC-013 R-52 as landed, so it is a
  bounded exception: only a task's own change, only with a confirmed
  `--into` line the task declares and that passes the trusted-line
  binding on either entry point, with main kept as policy,
  commit-history and completion-history authority.
- At the line-to-main landing, competing copies of a record meet in a
  Git merge, and the epic line range skips the frozen-criteria
  comparison, so today only the reviewer and the operator's merge see
  them. AC-9 adds a report there, at the level the operator decides. AC-16 carries the
  exception above; the R-52 sentence is a TSK-350 deliverable, changed
  through the planning change R-32 requires before the code.
- It moves a criteria-authority boundary, so the operator decides the
  whole (AC-6, AC-9 and AC-9's level); TSK-350 writes no AC-6 or AC-9
  code until that approval is recorded, and contrary AC-7 findings
  change the rule by amendment first.
- TSK-306's record, acceptance and tests stay unchanged; AC-8 requires
  its tests to pass.

## Printed project values amendment (2026-10-28)

The third review of TSK-1029 found that CodeFlow's printed diagnostics
can show what a project wrote: a TOML parser error quoted the offending
source line, and update's report printed workflow step names and policy
values. The same class reaches parser errors, semantic validation
messages, warnings and diagnostic reports in all three crates.

Ownership is split by output, with exactly one owner per reader and
printer, recorded in both task records against TSK-1029's head:

- TSK-1029 owns everything `codeflow update` and `codeflow update --pin`
  print and export, the readers they reach (including the policy, state,
  pending-record and CI pin readers they share with other commands), the
  location-only `ParsePlace` type and its helpers for TOML and JSON
  errors, and the version display rule both records state.
- TSK-1051, a follow-up of TSK-1029 in this epic under the standing
  direction that fixes go in the current release, owns every other
  reader and printer in the three crates and in every shipped asset
  that runs, including the docs portal starter scripts. It starts with an investigation (its AC-1): an
  inventory, tied to a revision, of input families, readers, commands,
  sinks and owners, which decides the build.

Graph v21 adds the edge TSK-1029 -> TSK-1051, because TSK-1051 builds on
TSK-1029's helpers and ownership contract; no other edge changes and no
epic criterion changes.

## Guard scope follow-ups amendment (2026-10-28)

On 2026-10-28 the operator approved the option D scope for TSK-1002
(PR 346): the text guard keeps every check on a path a command names,
and two classes leave its promise, a file run after a same-line write
and an unmodelled runner's child directory. The sandbox contains them
where containment is established; elsewhere a fail-closed rule refuses
them. The source is Fable's scope note `target/tsk1002-option-d-scope.md`
in the `guard-bypass-classes` worktree, with Codex review 04 and Fable
review 06 of PR 346 at `399594818`. The remaining findings of those
reviews move to two tasks in this epic, under the standing direction
that features and fixes go in the current release, with AC-152 and
AC-153, and AC-86's `cd` clause is superseded to match TSK-397's
design D. Codex reviews 01 and 02 of this plan (PR 390) and the
coordinator's decisions on them, under the operator's secure-by-default
rule, set the three rules below.

| Item | Task | Placement and reason |
|---|---|---|
| Runs after a same-line write fail closed on an unsandboxed host | TSK-1052 | the fail-closed rule wherever containment cannot be established; it reads TSK-397's `sandbox_state`, so it follows TSK-397, which is in this release |
| Runner directory grammars | TSK-1053 | lifts the inline-code over-refusals under runners that PR 346 introduces and judges reader words in the runner's directory; needs no TSK-397 work |
| AC-86's `cd` clause | this amendment | superseded by a dated sub-bullet: `cd src && python3 tool.py ..` refuses in the tightened outcome and takes design D's verdict, refuse, in the baseline; TSK-1052 builds on that outcome |

Rules both tasks follow:

- Containment: TSK-1052's rule applies wherever containment cannot be
  established, so the unknown-containment cases that design D leaves on
  the baseline rule (its A6: invisible overrides such as a `--settings`
  file, server-managed settings, MDM policy or a Codex bypass launch,
  and unrecognized retries such as Codex payloads) move from TSK-397's
  baseline to TSK-1052's fail-closed rule for this class. TSK-397's
  Part A stays as approved, and this amendment does not change its
  record.
- Execution identity: a command name stands for a program only when it
  resolves to an executable the requester cannot substitute, after
  same-line and inherited lookup state (`PATH`, hash bindings, aliases,
  functions, local package binaries); otherwise it is an unmodelled
  program. No allowance rests on a name alone.
- Verified runs: a same-line write with known bytes (`printf`, a literal
  heredoc) still triggers TSK-1052's rule; only a run the guard read and
  judged under the actual interpreter, identity and directory counts as
  verified.

Graph v22 adds the edge TSK-397 -> TSK-1052 and the root TSK-1053; no
other edge changes.

Notes:

- Both tasks are follow-ups of TSK-1002, which is standalone and whose
  record is not on this line yet. Both are blocked until PR 346 lands,
  as TSK-1035 is. The amendment that adds `depends_on: [TSK-1002]` to
  TSK-1022 and TSK-1035 after PR 346 lands adds the same edge to
  TSK-1052 (keeping TSK-397) and TSK-1053. Before it unblocks them, it
  verifies that TSK-1002's landed record names both in `follow_ups`, as
  section 4 of the scope note calls for; on 2026-10-28 neither PR 346's
  published record nor its local history at `c1dccabd2` did.
  `follow_up_of` is not set: for a standalone predecessor
  `task new --follow-up-of` files a standalone follow-up on a task
  branch (its help text), while the operator placed both in this epic.
- No dependency forces a later release: TSK-1052 needs TSK-397 and
  TSK-1002, and TSK-1053 needs TSK-1002; all three target this line.
- TSK-397's record on this line still states its outcome before option
  D. Its `sandbox_state` module, strict branch and replay harness are
  design D's H-6 and H-8 and arrive with its PR 331 rewrite; TSK-1052's
  deliverable homes are provisional until then. TSK-397's AC-1 still
  requires `cd src && python3 tool.py ..` to pass, against design D's
  AC-1; this amendment settles the epic side (AC-86 above), and PR 331
  rewrites TSK-397's record, which this amendment does not edit.
  TSK-1052 is admitted only after that rewrite has landed with TSK-397,
  which its `depends_on: [TSK-397]` edge enforces.
- No edge between TSK-1052 and TSK-1053: neither needs the other's
  code. Where both apply, TSK-1052's refusal prevails and TSK-397's
  strict branch is never relaxed by a runner grammar; TSK-1053's AC-8
  keeps every base refusal outside one reasoned list (runner reads and
  inline code with established identities, and, since 2026-10-29, the
  AC-15 `**` rows and AC-16 history reads that reach no protected path). `cargo run --` arguments
  are read as the built program's data, but the built program sits in
  writable build output, so its identity is not established and those
  rows keep their refusal with a corrected reason. The second of the
  two to land runs both tasks' fixtures in every containment case on
  the combined tree (TSK-1052 AC-8, TSK-1053 AC-10).
- `direnv exec` is not modelled: it does not change the child's
  directory, it discovers configuration from its argument and that
  directory's ancestors, and it resolves the child through the
  environment it loads (Codex review 02 of this plan, from direnv's
  v2.35.0 source). Text cannot establish that context, so under the
  operator's rule to remove what cannot be made sound it stays an
  unmodelled runner and keeps the unknown-directory rule.
- Release classification of TSK-1052 is the operator's, at build time,
  on AC-7's measured numbers; if breaking, the task carries `type!:`, a
  `BREAKING CHANGE:` migration footer, a major changelog fragment and
  `Impact: major` and `Breaking: yes` in its PR, and the release version
  follows the calculator.
- Hotspots, one writer at a time: `security/outward.rs`,
  `security/interpreter.rs` and `security/composed/paths.rs` (TSK-1052,
  TSK-1053, TSK-397, TSK-1035 and TSK-1024). These are file overlaps,
  not edges.
- Each task starts with an investigation criterion, listed first, by
  the operator's rule of 2026-10-27, its tests and journey judge each
  case through both git-guard and exec-guard, and each review is
  cross-lineage with the `cf-security-reviewer` checklist.
- TSK-1051 gains the `## Outcome` line TSK-435 (PR 364) now requires of
  a record created after adoption; without it `codeflow validate --docs`
  fails on the line tip `dc9ec266d`. The line restates its Description;
  no criterion changes.

## Session features fit amendment (2026-10-28)

A Fable review of how the away session (TSK-1045, TSK-1046), the
coordination ledger's follow-ups (EPC-025: TSK-439, TSK-440, TSK-423)
and the session sweep (EPC-023: TSK-1037, TSK-1041) fit together found
timing defaults that contradict each other, two homes for one fact and
two renderers for one view. This amendment fixes the records on this
line; no task joins or leaves, no epic criterion changes, and the graph
stays v23.

| Task | Change |
|---|---|
| TSK-1045 | `away tick` refreshes the heartbeat as a bound mutating operation (AC-9(g)); `away start` warns when the interval is not shorter than the heartbeat timeout (AC-2(e)); a follow-up added under an activation takes its interval as the next check, and an older build's `follow add` refusal names both schema versions (AC-3(e), (f)); the digest line counts against the 30-line guideline (AC-4); new AC-13 for an absent, damaged or read-only ledger; operator items render through one module, TSK-440's `coord/surface.rs`, and the fold rule now covers `coord status` |
| TSK-1046 | AC-1(b) decides a continuation mode inside TSK-440's one `Stop` entry, or none; `writing.md` gets one verb set (Run, Review, Approve, Decide, Provide, with Note), replacing Do, Confirm and Clarify, with a dated note on ADR-0071 (deliverables, AC-10(d)) |

Notes:

- Without these, an away coordinator ticking every 30 minutes would show
  as contact overdue to every stream, and each owed action would read
  overdue 20 minutes after each tick, against the ledger's 10-minute
  check and 30-minute heartbeat defaults (TSK-439 AC-6).

  confirms it with the away pair's placement.
- EPC-025's records (TSK-439 AC-6, TSK-440's fold rule and `Stop` edge,
  TSK-423's heading) change on the EPC-025 line, where their builders
  start; EPC-023's records change here, under that epic's own note.

that this line ships as 4.0.0, not 3.2.0. It absorbs the release the
2026-10-18 plan called 4.0.0 (the baseline replacement with its
incompatible manifest, and the read-only update plan from issues 74 and
119), so adopters migrate once. TSK-397 Part A and TSK-1052 ship in it
as breaking changes with migration guidance. 4.1.0 (EPC-023) and 4.2.0
(EPC-024) keep their numbers and content. This amendment moves no task,
changes no criterion and keeps the graph at v23.

Release condition: this release waits for the baseline replacement and
the read-only update plan. Their tasks and epic criteria do not exist on
this line yet; the coordinator owns the amendment that adds them, after
the readiness report of 2026-10-28, and this epic's pull request to
`main` is not ready until that amendment has landed and its tasks are
complete.

What changed:

- This epic's title, heading, summary, issue table, scope notes and
  release-impact note; TSK-1052's release impact (major, by the same
  decision).
- In open task records, every current statement that named 3.2.0 as this
  release: placements, edges, build and serial order, and "after the
  release ships" conditions. EPC-023 and EPC-024 records that point at
  this release likewise; EPC-024's release-slot note is rewritten.

What did not change, on purpose:

- The integration branch name, `integration/EPC-022-the-3-2-0-milestone-fixes`.
- Dated amendment sections and dated clauses, which report a decision,
  rule or review in the words of its day, such as "the operator's rule
  of 2026-10-18 (every security gap belongs in 3.2.0)". Read 3.2.0 in
  them as this release.
- Quotes, evidence naming a built binary's version (`codeflow 3.2.0
  source=...`), complete and cancelled records, version stamps
  (`Cargo.toml`, the managed-block markers, `.codeflow/manifest.json`),
  test data and scanner configuration. The version stamp moves in the
  release preparation pull request. Two pending changelog fragments
  name 3.2.0 (`changelog.d/TSK-302.md`, `changelog.d/TSK-377.md`); a
  planning pull request may not touch them, so they change in a task
  pull request or the release preparation.
- EPC-025's records, which change on the EPC-025 line where its builders
  start.
- The dated planning note on PR 125 and the dated sections that call
  the baseline replacement a later 4.0.0 release. The issue table, "Out
  of scope" and "Deferred work" now say it is in this release.
- Two statements were relabelled although they read as history, and
  are restored after Codex review 01 of PR 393: TSK-321's dated
  placement of TSK-322 and TSK-323, and completed TSK-433's record.

Two roots join this epic, from issue 394 and from a guard gap the

or criterion changes.

| TSK-1067 | AC-160 | exec-guard reads inline code only for python, node, nodejs, perl and ruby (`security/outward.rs:1391-1406`), so an `awk` program that writes an enforcement path through a literal output target, `system()` command or command pipe passes exec-guard; the builder reported git-guard refusing the literal output case, which the task's AC-1 reproduces |

## Issue 400 amendment (2026-10-29)

Issue #400 (CodeFlow 3.1.1): a merge of `main` into an integration line
that carries a task record through `blocked -> todo -> complete` is
refused by `codeflow ci` and the pre-push hook as `blocked -> complete`,
because `work.records` judges a range from the merge-base copy to the
head copy. The triage reproduced it on the line at `53749287c` with the
`35672e8bd` binary (docs and plan files only differ from the line, no
crate changes). Run with `codeflow ci` only: each of the two commits, judged as its own range, gave no `work.records` finding. The issue reports that pushing the sync in two steps passes. The pre-push hook was not run.

Placement: folded into TSK-1036 (open, `todo`, no pull request) as
AC-7, serving AC-144. TSK-1036 already owns the step-by-step judgment of
a `blocked -> todo -> complete` sequence in `judge_range_against`
(AC-3), so one writer changes the function once. Checked and not the
owner: TSK-340 (complete; the repeated-closeout sequence), TSK-433
(complete; a sync merge counted against a reviewed commit, issue #320),
TSK-1066 (a sync that hand-edits a task file) and TSK-421 (the
`codeflow line sync` command). No graph change.

Notes:

- The same triage found the reverse gap: `todo -> cancelled -> todo`
  in one range nets to `todo -> todo` and passes, although its second
  step alone is refused because a cancelled task is terminal. AC-7
  covers both directions, each with a failing-before fixture.
- As an argument about status transitions only, judging each step
  accepts only sequences that two separate pushes already accept; it is
  not proof that every other check passes. A merge is judged against
  every parent, even when its record matches one, so taking the other
  side's `todo` cannot revive a task cancelled on this side. Unreadable
  history refuses with a remedy for its cause (fetch for shallow
  history, repair the object store for a missing object, fix the
  record for an unparsable one) and never falls back to the endpoints.
  No default-branch tip or remote-tracking ref is trusted to exempt a
  step, and the guarantee covers the history supplied and verified.
- The change also makes `blocked -> todo -> complete` legal in a plain
  multi-commit range, which AC-3 already asks for.
- TSK-1036's `lifecycle.rs` citations are refreshed to `:1148` and
  `:2197`, and issue #400 joins its `external_refs`.

Two tasks join this epic: one adopts issue 402, which the operator decided
on 2026-10-29 (13:2x) to take as recommended, and one files the follow-up
that TSK-1063 recorded after its Codex review. The graph moves to v25 with
one root edge and one dependency edge; no other task or criterion changes.

| TSK-1070 | AC-161 | Issue 402 (related to issue 351 and TSK-1028, complete): the shipped status line gains `effort.level`, `pr.number` with `pr.review_state`, the 5-hour and 7-day `rate_limits` percentages and `workspace.git_worktree`, each shown only when Claude Code supplies it. The field names were checked against the Claude Code status line documentation on 2026-10-29 and match the issue. The docs keep saying a committed `statusLine` replaces a contributor's own and that `.claude/settings.local.json` is the opt-out; `codeflow update` moves a line that is exactly a shipped value by TSK-1028 AC-4's rule; the no-`jq` fallback stays |

The task makes CI report shard drift before a shard overruns, or records why it cannot and which check catches the drift instead |

Notes:

- TSK-1070 has no edge. TSK-1028 and TSK-1029 are complete and their code
  is on this line, so the new line builds on what exists. TSK-1028's
  criteria are unchanged. Open tasks write the same files, with one writer

## Issue 404 amendment (2026-10-29)

Issue 404 reports false refusals of three kinds: quoted data read as
code or paths (kind 1), a `$VAR` judged unresolved although the line
assigns it (kind 2), and refusals that come from what a rule means
(kind 3). It asks for a real shell parser. Each row was rebuilt from
its description (the issue gives no exact lines) and run through both
guards of the line's binary at `bd721f39b`. Two tasks join this epic:
TSK-1072 with AC-163, and the parser, TSK-1073, with AC-164. TSK-1024
gains AC-9, AC-10 and two dated notes, TSK-415 gains AC-8, and TSK-1053
gains a note and AC-17. Graph v27 adds `TSK-397 -> TSK-1072` and
TSK-397, TSK-415, TSK-417, TSK-1053 and TSK-1072 -> TSK-1073; PR 403's
v25 edges are unchanged. (v26 existed only on this planning branch.)

Session rows of the issue:

| Row | Kind | At `bd721f39b` | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| `git -C "$W" show` | 2 | passes when the line assigns `W`; refused when it does not (Git runner reader, both guards) | TSK-406 (complete) for the assigned form; TSK-1024 AC-9 keeps the unassigned refusal |
| `git -C "$R" ...`, `git.policy_authority` | 2 | as above (`log`, `ls-remote`, `fetch --dry-run` checked) | as above |
| `sed -i` under `$D` | 2 | passes when assigned; refused by git-guard when not | TSK-406 (complete); the unassigned refusal stays |
| `tar -xf $D/main.tar -C $D/main-tree` | 2 | passes when assigned | TSK-406 (complete) |
| `python3 -I -c` with a regex naming `sudo` | 1 | refused (the privilege family's code-word check) | none: interpreter code is scanned on purpose; TSK-1073 AC-6 keeps it |
| heredoc Python holding `; subprocess.run` | 1 | refused (`; su` substring) | TSK-1072 |
| `sed` with a shell-filled word, then Python with a quoted brace | 1 | refused (`git.hook_integrity`, the 4096-entry glob) | TSK-415 AC-4 and AC-8, TSK-417 AC-2 and AC-4 |
| heredoc Python naming a peer's headless form | 1 | refused (`security.headless_peer_runs`) | none: interpreter code; TSK-1073 AC-6 keeps it |

Tracker rows of the issue, and one row reported by two agents on
2026-10-29:

| Line | Kind | At `bd721f39b` | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| 439, a grep pattern and `rm` | 1 | passes | TSK-380 (complete) |
| 505, an append quoting a git command | 1 | passes (`echo` form) | none needed |
| 506, `sed` with a quoted pattern | 1 | refused | TSK-415 AC-8 |
| 601, a heredoc and `chmod` | 1 | passes | none needed; running the file stays TSK-1052's class |
| 774 and 808, `sed` with a shell-filled word | 2 | passes alone; refused beside another command's quoted brace | TSK-415, TSK-417 |
| 945 and 1315, heredoc writes naming a peer's headless form | 1 | passes through `cat`, `tee` and `gh pr comment --body`; refused only in interpreter code (session row 8) | none needed |
| 1062, `git branch -r --list` with a glob | 3 | refused (`git.discard_uncommitted`) | TSK-1024 AC-1 |
| 1354, `; su` in prose and a regex with `$'` | 1 | passes through `printf` and `grep`; refused by exec-guard as `gh pr comment --body` data and in a `tee` heredoc | TSK-1073 |
| 209, a transcript grep | 1 | passes | none needed |
| `gh pr merge --help` and `-h` | 3 | the target is empty, so git-guard looks up the current branch's pull request base: refused (`git.pr_merge_to_protected`) when that base is protected or the lookup fails, as in a network-denied sandbox; passes when it is unprotected; `gh help pr merge` passes | TSK-1024 AC-10 |

Notes:

- Placement. TSK-1072 is a bounded matcher fix that forces a workaround
  today, so it goes in 4.0.0 under the standing direction. TSK-1073 is
  in 4.0.0 by the operator's decision of 2026-10-29. This pull request
  first recommended 4.1.0 (EPC-023), because the parser needs a new
  dependency, execution identity and a qualification corpus run under
  real shells; the operator chose the current release, and the
  qualification ships with the task (its AC-3). The EPC-023 criterion
  and graph change first drafted for it never reached the line, so
  nothing published is removed. Its edges put it after TSK-397,
  TSK-415, TSK-417, TSK-1053 and TSK-1072, and its corpus keeps their
  fixtures; TSK-1072's edge makes its base verdict include the
  whole-word matcher, since both write `security/privilege.rs`. It
  shares the guard modules with the other open guard tasks, one writer
  at a time.
- TSK-1024 AC-9: the runner reader refuses a read-only `git` whose
  repository is filled in at run time, because it cannot read that
  repository's configuration, which can run a program (TSK-1065). AC-2
  could be read as asking to lift that; AC-9 and the two dated notes
  keep it, a security decision under the operator's secure-by-default
  rule of 2026-10-24.
- TSK-1024 AC-10: a help form is TSK-1024's kind of refusal (a command
  that changes nothing, refused by git-guard), and no open task owns
  `check_gh_pr_merge`. The refusal depends on the base lookup an empty
  target triggers (`hook.rs:675`), so the criterion's tests fix that
  lookup to a protected base and to a failed one; every real merge,
  bare `gh pr merge` and `--delete-branch` stay refused.
- TSK-1053's note and AC-17 carry Fable review 14 of PR 346: a Git
  program variable (`GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF='cat ~/.codex/auth.json'`) before
  `lazygit`, `cargo test`, `git status` or `git diff` passes both guards
  at `bd721f39b`. AC-17 requires both guards to judge the program it
  names, or refuse. The coordinator sent the class to PR 346's
  (TSK-1002) fix batch on 2026-10-29, where it is expected to close;
  AC-17 is the backstop if it does not. PR 346 may not change another
  task's record (ADR-0078), so the planning amendment adds it.
- The issue's request to track `cd`, `pushd` and `popd` is not taken:
  the operator decided on 2026-10-25 that narrowing after `cd` cannot be
  made sound (TSK-397), and TSK-1073 keeps every directory change
  unknown. The issue's two quoted limits stay for that reason.
- PRs 331 and 346 are open. TSK-1072 extends TSK-397's replay harness,
  hence its edge. TSK-1073 builds on TSK-397's lexer, which stays as its
  divergence check and is not replaced, and on the execution-identity
  rule TSK-1053 implements; TSK-1002's edge is added with TSK-1052's and
  TSK-1053's after PR 346 lands. TSK-1073 is `todo` with blocked
  predecessors; it is admitted only when they complete.
- Grok reviews 01 and 02 of PR 405 corrected this amendment: the
  `GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF` boundary (TSK-1053 AC-17), the base lookup behind
  the `gh pr merge --help` refusal (TSK-1024 AC-10), the `tee` and

- Shared files, one writer at a time: `security/privilege.rs` and

## Session design amendment (2026-10-29)

The away pair (TSK-1045, TSK-1046) is amended to the session system
design, revision 12.3 of 2026-10-29, the last design revision. Codex
(GPT-6 Astra at high) reviewed revisions 8 to 11 (r11-04: SOUND) and,
under the corrected threat model, 12.1 and 12.2 (r12-05 and r12-06,
both NOT SOUND). Revision 12.3 turns r12-06's blocking finding into the
named landing residual and its races into fifteen fault tests TSK-1045
must pass; no Codex review covers 12.3 itself. The
design trusts the session's own tool results as evidence, read
defensively, and so runs these unattended: claiming units, building
and reviewing, replacing a lost seat on a fresh branch and worktree,
landing reviewed and gated units into integration lines, and renewing
and cleaning the activation's own scheduled job. Landing into `main`,
clearing a landing mismatch, releasing a quarantined worktree, the
first arming, a second activation, a rebind after `/branch` and
anything after a reboot stay attended. Graph v28 replaces
`TSK-439 -> TSK-1045` with `TSK-1038 -> TSK-1045` and
`TSK-1047 -> TSK-1045` and keeps PR 405's v27 edges; no other edge
changes. PR 407 is open and also numbers EPC-022 v28 and EPC-023 v13.
It lands after this one, renumbers both graphs past these and keeps both
edge sets, so `TSK-439 -> TSK-1045` stays removed.

| Task | Change |
|---|---|
| TSK-1045 | one activation per clone in `codeflow/coord/away.json`, owned by one Claude Code session, with owed items in the activation instead of ledger follow-ups; the lock contract for every effect; the claim under the cooperative claim contract (AC-14); ticks admitted by nonce and spacing (AC-15); seat transitions, snapshot, evidence states, fresh-target replacement and quarantine (AC-16, AC-17); the owner identity walk (AC-18); clock rules (AC-19); arming, renewal and owned-job cleanup (AC-20, AC-21); landing admission and verification (AC-22, AC-23); `away start --from` (AC-24); the `security_paths` policy key (AC-25); fault tests F1 to F15 (AC-26); the contracts and preconditions in the docs (AC-27). The ledger schema change, the stream lease, the heartbeat checks and the usage limit are removed, each with its reason in the record |
| TSK-1046 | the skill claims, builds, reviews, replaces seats, lands into lines and renews its job in scheduled turns within purpose (AC-3); a merge into `main` stays an Approve item (AC-5); a security-path unit waits for another lineage; the attended steps go to the user (AC-14); new qualification cases for a moved head, a reduced-assurance review, a replacement after a usage limit, a duplicate job and a re-armed job |
| TSK-1047 | edge note: TSK-1045 now depends on it |
| TSK-1038 | edge note: TSK-1045 now depends on it |

Notes:

- The TSK-439 edge is removed because the activation holds no ledger
  role: a fresh adopter records an activation through the shipped CLI
  before TSK-439 lands. The Away session amendment's paragraph that
  dispatches TSK-1045 only after TSK-439 reaches this line is
  superseded. Moving owed items to ledger follow-ups once the away
  owner can bind as coordinator is an EPC-025 note, made on the
  EPC-025 line.
- TSK-1045 depends on TSK-1047 and TSK-1038 because `work claim
  --away` claims from the base TSK-1047's rule sets and from a ready
  set that, after TSK-1038, never offers landed work.
- The design's four preconditions, accepted by the operator on
  2026-10-29 (relayed by the coordinator):
  - ruleset C on `integration/**`, strict and without bypass actors for
    the merging identity, written by the content-policy design's unit
    U5. That unit's task is not filed yet, so this is a dated note:
    when its planning pull request files the task, an amendment adds it
    as a predecessor of the away pair's landing journeys. Until then
    TSK-1045 refuses every landing and owes the configuration, so
    building does not wait for it;
  - the cooperative claim contract (TSK-1045 AC-14, AC-27);
  - the exact-copy watcher contract (TSK-1045 AC-21, AC-27);
  - `security_paths` as a new policy key. No task owns the key, so
    TSK-1045 AC-25 adds it. It fails closed in four cases, each with a
    test that fails if the classifier matches nothing: a missing key, a
    malformed key or an unparseable policy applies the default list; an
    unreadable line-tip policy refuses the landing; an empty list `[]`
    is invalid and applies the default list (decided by the coordinator
    under the operator's standing secure-by-default rule of 2026-10-24,
    not an operator question). Separately, an unattended candidate that
    changes `security_paths` in any way is refused.
- AC-150 note (2026-10-29): "take no operator-owned step" covers a
  merge into `main` and the attended steps above; landing into an
  integration line under TSK-1045's admission checks is the primary's
  ordinary step. The criterion's wording is unchanged.
- The design's ten residuals, accepted by the operator on 2026-10-29
  (relayed by the coordinator): the landing
  destination is not bound at the merge (C4, E1, E2: a retarget in the
  final window is caught after and blocks, not prevented); an effect
  already dispatched at a stop can complete (A18); a reboot ends the
  activation (O2); an escaped seat is outside the activation's reach
  (P2); and four deferred rows (E9, H2, H3, H7).
- What the design still defers is tracked once in EPC-023 (its Session
  design amendment of this date): TSK-1074 for Codex-owned activations
  and trigger kinds with no list surface (H2, H3, E9); TSK-1075 for
  cross-clone claim arbitration in the title-slug case and TSK-1076 for
  usage as a stop trigger, both kept by the design's records list. H7,
  the delegate heartbeat and Stop lifecycle, stays with TSK-440
  (EPC-025). The three are blocked and attach to EPC-023, so none holds
  this epic's close.
- Autonomous landing, seat replacement and unattended renewal are inside
  TSK-1045 and TSK-1046, so no separate record is filed for them.
- TSK-1045 carries 27 criteria for one outcome; the design keeps the
  engine one task because every effect shares one store, lock contract
  and reconciliation. A split, if the builder's investigation shows one,
  is a batched epic amendment.
- TSK-1037 (EPC-023) is unchanged: `codeflow sweep` wraps TSK-1045's
  reader for an attended session with no activation.

## Content policy amendment (2026-10-29)

Issue #279 asked for a repository content policy. CodeFlow's response is
a design by Claude Fable 5.1 (revision 5, finalized 2026-10-29 22:30Z)
built from Claude's analysis, Fable's review, Grok 4.7's first-principles
design and Grok's reviews 02 to 04. It found fourteen real defects, among
them two high: any workflow can produce a required check name under the
Actions app id, and the secret scan is the pull request's own workflow.
The operator decided all eleven of its decisions on 2026-10-29, among them
Mode A (decision 3), a dedicated GitHub App that alone creates the
default branch's required policy checks. This section files the 4.0.0
fixes; the 4.2.0 feature changes are in EPC-024's section of the same
name.

| Task | Criterion | What it covers |
|---|---|---|
| TSK-1077 (new, root) | AC-165 | the default branch at one SHA is the only local policy authority; line-sourced exceptions, the `origin/main` guess and the empty-namespace and pre-push fallbacks are removed; the ADR for the whole model |
| TSK-1078 (new) | AC-166 | pre-commit secret and conflict-marker scans run at the landed level, closing Grok's unowned finding D-1 |
| TSK-1079 (new) | AC-167 | symlinks and gitlinks at exempt paths are judged, and `credentials.json` is blocked (D-2, D-12) |
| TSK-1080 (new) | AC-168 | `remote protect` writes rulesets A, B and C it owns; C is strict with no bypass for any identity (D-11, D-13) |
| TSK-336 (amended) | AC-33, AC-93 | both hosted jobs run only the default branch's code; `trusted:` verdict jobs; the App's `authority:` checks (Mode A) over Mode B's floor; the head-run `secret scan` and `release impact` jobs removed |
| TSK-323 (amended) | AC-69 | size and ignored-file rules on by default, with the admission routes (decision 8) |
| TSK-1057 (amended, EPC-023) | none here | its baseline binds TSK-1077's authority; the remote-removal and `symbolic-ref` routes are unauthorized refreshes |
| TSK-1036 (note) | none | the interim cost of a `main` sync until its AC-7 is on `main` |
| TSK-1030 (note) | none | two one-clause AC-2 edits owed in its open PR 376 |
| TSK-1045 and TSK-1046 (notes) | none | their ruleset C precondition now points at TSK-1080, with no task edge |

Graph v29 adds `START -> TSK-1077`, `TSK-1077 -> TSK-1078`,
`START -> TSK-1079` and `START -> TSK-1080`, and replaces
`START -> TSK-336` with `TSK-1077 -> TSK-336`; it keeps every edge of
v28 (PR 405's issue 404 edges and PR 406's session design edges).
EPC-023's graph v14 adds `TSK-1077 -> TSK-1057` and keeps PR 406's v13
edges. Both stay acyclic: TSK-1077 has no predecessor, and nothing it
reaches leads back to it. AC-165 to AC-168 follow PR 405's AC-163 and
AC-164 without renumbering.

```text
TSK-1077 local authority ──> TSK-1078 pre-commit landed levels
       │
       ├──> TSK-336 hosted authority ──> (operator: Mode A setup,
       │                                  remote protect, retire
       │                                  24379074 and 25435656)
       └──> TSK-1057 baseline (EPC-023)
TSK-1079 path gate links     TSK-1080 rulesets A, B, C
TSK-323 (feat) and TSK-1030 (docs) alongside, no edge
```

Ruleset C and the away pair: the Session design amendment above names
ruleset C, strict with no bypass for the merging identity, as a
precondition of TSK-1045's and TSK-1046's unattended landing. TSK-1080
writes it; both records now point at it. No task edge is added: the
landing journeys need a forge where ruleset C exists (on this
repository, after the operator's enablement run), not TSK-1080's code,
and until then the activation refuses every landing (TSK-1045
AC-22(a)), so both tasks build and test without it. This supersedes
that amendment's wording that the unit's task is not filed yet and that
an amendment adds it as a predecessor of the away pair's landing
journeys: TSK-1080 is filed, and there is no `TSK-1080 -> TSK-1045`
edge, for the reason above. The landed paragraph is left as written.

Placement and order:

- All four new tasks and the amended TSK-336 are fixes in 4.0.0, each
  with fail-before and pass-after per criterion and the
  `cf-security-reviewer` checklist on the review. Builders: Codex GPT-6
  Astra at high (TSK-1078 and TSK-1079: GPT-6.1 Sol); reviewer Claude
  Fable 5.1 at high; Grok 4.7 where both lineages author.
- TSK-323 stays a feature on this line by the operator's placement of
  2026-10-18 and is not relabelled.
- TSK-336 must reach `main` before the operator runs `remote protect`
  (the enablement order). From that merge until the operator's step 6 in
  TSK-1080's Description ends, every pull request into `main` and the
  lines is unmergeable, fail closed by design.
- TSK-1079 and TSK-1080 have no edge. TSK-1080's hosted fixtures share a
  scratch repository and the App fixture with TSK-336, one owner at a
  time, never this repository.

Hotspots, one writer at a time:

- `landed_policy.rs` and `push_set.rs`: TSK-1077, then TSK-1057;
  `landed_policy.rs` is also TSK-330's and TSK-320's.
- `remote.rs`: TSK-1080.
- Both workflow sets: TSK-336.
- `crates/codeflow-cli/src/cmd/ci.rs`: TSK-1077 (the proven-absence
  rule at `:1986`), then TSK-336 (the workflow-change finding and the
  report lines); the edge `TSK-1077 -> TSK-336` already orders them.
- `path_policy.rs`: TSK-323 and TSK-1079 in 4.0.0, one writer at a time,
  then TSK-428 in 4.2.0. No edge between TSK-323 and TSK-1079: neither
  needs the other's behaviour (TSK-1079 moves the symlink and gitlink
  rule ahead of the exemption; TSK-323 adds the size and ignore rules,
  which never judge exempt inputs), so whichever lands second rebases on
  the other.

Public behaviour changes for the 4.0.0 notes (workspace
`RELEASE-PLAN.md:105` labels this release 4.0.0):

- pull requests into integration lines are judged by the default
  branch's code and policy, locally and hosted (decision 4 accepts that a
  changed `codeflow ci` check is exercised by the hosted job only after it
  lands on `main`);
- a project that keeps real exceptions or levels only on a line loses
  them (decision 5; none in this repository);
- `remote protect` writes ruleset C on the lines: pull request required,
  strict, no direct push, no force push, no deletion, no bypass; line
  creation stays unrestricted (decision 6, and the operator's decision of
  2026-10-27 21:40);
- the agent principal's permission set: Contents and Pull requests write
  and Actions read, with no Workflows, Commit statuses, Checks,
  Administration, Environments or secrets access (decision 1);
- the size and ignored-file defaults (decision 8, TSK-323).

Owed by the operator, not agent work: the Mode A GitHub steps and the
enablement run listed in TSK-1080's Description (create and install the
App, the `codeflow-authority` environment with its key and id, then,
after TSK-336 is on `main`, `remote protect --check-app <id> --lines
"integration/**"`, retirement of rulesets 24379074 and 25435656 and a
green `doctor --check remote-perimeter`), and decision 1's agent
principal at any time.

Residuals the ADR (TSK-1077 AC-7) and every report state: forgery of
Actions-pinned checks by a workflow writer (the operator alone under
decision 1); public lines publish a task branch's content before any
check, so secrecy rests on the scans; the recovery and enablement
windows; the App key's reach to default-branch code; hostile regular
files at exempt executable paths; the local authority before TSK-1057;
the shared credential until decision 1's principal exists.

Notes:

- The new tasks start with an investigation criterion, numbered after
  the design's criteria and listed first, so the design's numbering
  stays traceable; amended tasks get one the same way, by the operator's
  rule of 2026-10-27.
- TSK-1057 stays in EPC-023 (decided 2026-10-29): it is the baseline
  half of the 4.1.0 qualification and its integration target is already
  this line, so moving it here would only let an unfinished baseline hold
  the 4.0.0 close; its link to this work is the edge from TSK-1077.
- Not verified at planning, each owed by a fixture in TSK-336 AC-3 or
  TSK-1080 AC-2 to AC-4: GitHub's choice among same-named check runs;
  whether `update` refuses a non-bypass writer's merge; whether a ruleset
  `PUT` that omits `bypass_actors` keeps the list; the Workflows refusal
  for an App's merge; whether a Contents-write App can edit an
  environment; the App check flow and key boundary per trigger; squash
  and rebase checks on a line; fork and retarget behaviour.

This epic, TSK-283, TSK-395, TSK-407, TSK-415, TSK-417, TSK-1022,

branch, so it lands with that PR.

  text rule the default. PR 331's binary at `c53591264` judged the issue
  lines with the command text on stdin, never run, in a fixture whose
  origin policy is strict and one whose origin policy is `relaxed`.
  Exec-guard, which applies the text rule:

  `git.hook_integrity`, because TSK-415 and TSK-417, which fix those

  | Form | Criteria |
  |---|---|
  | `REV=HEAD; git log -1 $REV`, `git log -1 $(git rev-parse HEAD)` | TSK-1024 AC-3 |
  | a run-time `cd` or `-C` operand assigned earlier on the line (`S=<repo>; cd $S ...`, `W=<repo>; git -C "$W" show`) | TSK-1024 AC-2, AC-6 and AC-9 |
  | `cd $TMPDIR && rm -rf cfsrc` | TSK-1024 AC-5 |
  | `rm -rf ~/scratch/x`, `rm ~/.sshx-notes`, `rm ~/.cargo/config.toml` | TSK-1022 AC-5 |
  | `cat ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `printf x > ~/notes.txt` | TSK-395 AC-3 and AC-4 |

  Each gets the split with a dated note. The unassigned `cd $S` and
  `git -C "$W"` forms stay refused as TSK-1024 AC-9 states: git-guard
  refuses them under `git.hook_integrity`, or `git.policy_authority` for
  `remote show`, in both outcomes. `rm -rf ./target` and `rm ./*.log`
  pass in both outcomes. TSK-1067 AC-3 gets a dated note: putting
  unresolved `awk` program text on the interpreter rule is that task's
  change, in both outcomes. PR 331's binary refuses the `awk` forms
  under `security.text_guard` under the strict default, inside a
  checkout and outside one, and passes them under relaxed, where
  `python3 -c "$UNSET"` gets `git.hook_integrity` inside a checkout and
  passes outside one. AC-96 does not cover `awk`.
- TSK-283's security follow-up F3 records that TSK-1082 (PR 408, landed
  at `312ea0216`) resolved its consequence: a stalled guard now refuses
  at its own 20 second deadline. Discovery stays unbounded.
- Track once: `ls <store> | xargs -I{} cat <store>/{}` is refused under
  `security.dangerous_commands` with a message about a recursive deletion
  whose target cannot be proven. The refusal is right; the message names
  deletion for a read (PR 346, TSK-1002, Fable review 15). Revisit at the
  next change to the `xargs` deletion reader, and make the message name
  the read.

## After TSK-1002 amendment (2026-10-30)

TSK-1002 landed through PR 346 (merge `6a05e1c00`). This amendment files
what it left and one new issue. Two tasks join this epic, AC-169 and
AC-170 serve them, and graph v30 adds `START -> TSK-1083` and
`START -> TSK-1084`; no existing edge changes. TSK-1065 (EPC-023) is
amended.

| Task | Criterion | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| TSK-1083 (new) | AC-169 | Issue #413: a landed task whose record was added on the line binds at its reviewed head, under fix A's conditions |
| TSK-1084 (new) | AC-170 | The `hooks_cli` guard verdicts stop depending on the host (PR 346's hosted failure at `92e7b5029`) |
| TSK-1065 (amended) | none here | Narrowed to what TSK-1002 left: programs the guard cannot read from the line and executables it cannot trust |

- TSK-1083 binds trust, so Fable reviews its design and the build gets a
  cross-lineage review with the `cf-security-reviewer` checklist. It
  shares `acceptance.rs` with TSK-1066 and TSK-421; one writer at a time.
- TSK-1084 is a new task, not a track-once note: one `hooks_cli`
  verdict passed on macOS and failed on the Linux and Windows runners
  (run 41026268442) because its fixture configured no pager, the cause
  of the macOS pass is not yet known, a host-dependent verdict can hide
  a real verdict change, and no open record owns the harness.
- TSK-1065 stays in EPC-023 and stays blocked on TSK-1055. The line
  binary at `6a05e1c00` refuses the four text forms its AC-2 named, so
  AC-2 becomes a regression guard. It still allows a script-path
  program (`git -c core.pager=./pager.sh log`) and a substituted
  executable (`PATH=/private/tmp/evil:$PATH git -c core.pager=less log`),
  which AC-3 and AC-4 keep.
- Track once: a backup copy of a file a suffix pattern names as secret
  (`*.pem`, `*.key`, `*.p12`, `*.pfx`, for example `x.pem.bak`) is not
  treated as secret. This is TSK-1002's stated limit (option A, decided
  2026-10-30): one strip-suffix rule would refuse ordinary names such as
  `certificate.pem.txt` and `private.key.rs` (TSK-335), and a list of
  backup suffixes per pattern is patching. Revisit at the next change to
  that stated limit: its text in `docs/policy-reference.md:215-225` or
  its matcher, `secret_backup` in
  `crates/codeflow-core/src/security/interpreter.rs:1464-1479`.
- Track once: a `find -name` walk refuses with "cannot read paths
  reached through a glob" when an entry its selector excludes is
  read-denied under the root. Reproduction: a directory holding `a.md`
  and a sandbox-denied `sub/.env`; `find . -name '*.md' -exec cat {} +`
  refuses on the line binary, and `65f9ee12b` allowed it (PR 346, Fable
  review 18, Minor B). It fails closed, so it does not block. Revisit at
  the next change to the `find` walker or with TSK-1073.
- The two track-once items stay in this section, beside the `xargs`
  item above: the code each names was TSK-1002's, which is complete, so
  no open record owns it, and each is a stated limit or a fail-closed
  over-refusal whose revisit is a future change.

## Baseline replacement amendment (2026-10-30)

This amendment adds the tasks the Version relabel amendment owed: the
baseline replacement (issue 119's manifest portion) and the read-only
update plan and procedure (issue 74). Four tasks are new, TSK-247 joins
this epic, TSK-258 is cancelled, `SPC-015` is allocated, AC-171 and
AC-172 are added, and graph v31 adds the edges below. No existing
criterion or edge changes.

What the operator decided, and where it is recorded (`RELEASE-PLAN.md`):

| Decision | Date | Content |
|---|---|---|
| Option A | 2026-10-15 | An incompatible manifest and a major release, shipped as 4.0.0 |
| Sparse pack | 2026-10-15 | A tracked sparse pack beside the manifest is accepted |
| Retention | 2026-10-17 | The binary keeps the shipped bases of every published release (about 0.6 MB then); an advisory 2 MB budget triggers a review when crossed |
| Version | 2026-10-28 | This line ships as 4.0.0 and absorbs the replacement and the update plan, so adopters migrate once |

The design Codex approved on 2026-10-15 after five rounds
(`.worktrees/baseline-design/target/design-baseline-05.md`) was build
output in a worktree that no longer exists; no copy survives on disk, in a
ref or in a pull request (searched 2026-10-30). TSK-1085 re-derives it
from the three decisions and the current code into an accepted ADR and
the approved `SPC-015`, cross-lineage reviewed, before any code task
starts. If the operator holds a copy of the approved design, TSK-1085
shrinks to recording it.

| Task | Outcome | Size | Builder | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TSK-1085 (new) | The design is an accepted ADR and the approved `SPC-015` with numbered requirements and the measured pack size | medium, records only | Claude Fable 5.1 at high | Grok 4.7 at high |
| TSK-1086 (new) | The binary carries the shipped bases of v2.0.0 to v3.1.1 and the release step adds each new release's; the budget is reported | medium | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Grok 4.7 at high |
| TSK-1087 (new) | Update, init, orphans and doctor read bases from the binary and the sparse pack; the manifest has no digest; `.codeflow/.baseline/` is removed in one `codeflow update`; breaking, `feat(scaffold)!:` with migration | large | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Grok 4.7 at high, with the `cf-security-reviewer` checklist |
| TSK-247 (joins) | `codeflow update --plan` previews read-only: actions, references, overridden defaults, mirrors, the person's write list | large | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Grok 4.7 at high, with the `cf-security-reviewer` checklist for the write preflight |
| TSK-1088 (new) | `.codeflow/rules/updating.md` at every tier, the file-kind table in `docs/adoption.md`, `[scaffold] ignore` documented, the `cf-customize` trigger | small to medium, docs and assets | Claude Sonnet 5.5 at high | Grok 4.7 at high |

Codex is at its weekly limit as this amendment is written; every reviewer
seat above is probed at dispatch and Codex GPT-6 Astra at high takes the
review where it is available.

```text
START -> TSK-1085
TSK-1085 -> TSK-1086
TSK-1086 -> TSK-1087
TSK-397 -> TSK-1087
TSK-1052 -> TSK-1087
TSK-401 -> TSK-1087
TSK-1087 -> TSK-247
TSK-247 -> TSK-1088
```

- The chain is serial by design: the spec before the pack, the pack
  before the switch, the switch before the preview that reads its bases,
  the preview before the procedure that reads its output. TSK-1086 is its
  own unit because its pack layout and release step are the contract
  TSK-1087 consumes and the two together exceed one thorough review.
- TSK-1087 lands late: it rewrites every manifest entry and deletes the
  317-file baseline tree, so every open pull request that resyncs managed
  assets conflicts with it. Its edges name the operator's breaking peers
  (TSK-397 through PR 331, TSK-1052) and the other `update.rs` writer
  (TSK-401); the primary sequences it after every other asset-changing
  unit of this release that is ready when it is, and a unit landing after
  it resyncs with its build.
- Breaking changes and migration: TSK-1087 carries `feat(scaffold)!:`,
  a `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer and a major changelog fragment whose
  migration is: upgrade every checkout and CI pin to 4.0.0 together, run
  `codeflow update` once, commit the manifest and the baseline removal;
  a 3.x binary cannot read the result and fails closed. TSK-397 Part A
  and TSK-1052 carry their own, per the Version relabel amendment. The
  version stamp still moves in the release preparation pull request.
- Settled here, not asked: `codeflow update` removes `.codeflow/.baseline/`
  in the same run that rewrites the manifest (engine-generated content,
  ADR-0011's orphan rule; reversible through version control) rather than
  leaving it for the adopter to delete; the retained releases are every
  published tag (v2.0.0, v2.1.0, v3.0.0, v3.1.0, v3.1.1) plus each release
  from 4.0.0 on, per the retention decision.
- TSK-247 keeps its id and uid: its record was allocated on 2026-10-06 and
  never landed (local branch `task/TSK-247-update-plan` at `715bcc5f8`,
  one record-only commit on a history from 2026-10-06, worktree
  `.worktrees/update-plan`), and every record on this line that names the
  read-only plan names TSK-247. It becomes an epic task on this line; its
  former AC-5 moves to TSK-1087 AC-7 and its former AC-7 to TSK-1088,
  each with a dated note. Its code must be built fresh: the branch holds
  no code, and `update.rs` has since gained TSK-405's pending resolution
  and TSK-401's additions. The coordinator removes that branch and
  worktree once this amendment lands.
- TSK-258 (PR 125, draft into `main` since 2026-10-08, head `4b73a9de3`,
  ten commits on a `main` of 2026-10-08) is cancelled as superseded: its
  digest-free schema 2 kept `.codeflow/.baseline/`, the intervening format
  the 2026-10-18 note ruled out. Its tests and journeys (A1 and A2 merge
  trees, the 3.0.0 project update, the 3.0.0 binary on a schema 2
  project) are the reference for TSK-1087 AC-2 and AC-5. PR 125 is closed
  by the coordinator with a link to TSK-1087 once this amendment lands;
  its code is not merged.
- Release condition, restated: this epic's pull request to `main` is not
  ready until TSK-1085, TSK-1086, TSK-1087, TSK-247 and TSK-1088 are
  complete and AC-171's journey has run on the final candidate.
- Shared files, one writer at a time: `update.rs` (TSK-401, TSK-1087,
  TSK-247); `scripts/release.py` (TSK-1086, TSK-1087, the release
  preparation); `docs/adoption.md` "Update" (TSK-1087, TSK-247,
  TSK-1088); `assets/base/scaffold-manifest.toml` (TSK-1088 and every
  asset task).
- Follow-up recorded, no task: generating `.agents/skills` from
  `.claude/skills` so a project's skill edit is made once (issue 74 item
  4); an ADR-sized change for a later release.
- Not changed here: EPC-024's TSK-429 note that names "the 4.0.0 baseline
  replacement (TSK-247)" reads as TSK-1087; it is corrected in EPC-024's
  next amendment.
- Amended 2026-10-30: this epic no longer lists `SPC-015` in `specs`.
  An epic's specs are inherited by every task, and `work start`,
  `codeflow ci` and the pre-push hook refuse a task whose inherited spec
  is still draft (`work.stable_planning_anchor`), so every EPC-022 task
  was refused once this amendment landed. TSK-1085 produces the spec
  and no longer lists it as an input; TSK-1086, TSK-1087 and TSK-247
  keep it, since they start only after TSK-1085 approves it.

where they are ready together; the amendment after PR 438 lands writes those
edges. TSK-446 keeps its scope (PR 438 amends its record), and TSK-1114
depends on it instead of folding it. The audit's item 8, a visual acceptance
review in every unit's pull request, adds a review step to every unit of this
body and is the operator's decision: its protection is that a page is judged

## Policy authority ADR listing amendment (2026-10-31)

This amendment moves one clause out of TSK-1077. Its AC-7 required EPC-022
and EPC-023 to list the ADR that pull request 441 adds (ADR-0091, the
default branch at one commit as the only policy authority). A task pull
request cannot amend an epic record (`work.criteria_frozen`), and
`codeflow validate --docs` refuses an epic `adrs` entry whose ADR file is
not yet on the line: "epic references ADR-0091 but no matching file exists
in docs/decisions/". TSK-1077's AC-7 now ends at the ADR and the doc
diff; nothing else in it changes. No edge, owner or other criterion
changes.

Owed after pull request 441 lands: the first planning amendment after
that merge adds ADR-0091 to the `adrs` list of EPC-022 and of EPC-023.
Revisit event: pull request 441 merging into this line.

## Qualification runner worktree peers amendment (2026-10-31)

The native qualification runner refuses every peer seat a subject opens
outside the fixture repository root (`check_peer_state`,
`evals/qualification/runner.py:1302-1303` at TSK-1094's reviewed head
`addf25865`). The method puts a review seat in the subject's task
worktree, so a subject that follows it gets `peer_launch_refused` and its
trial is invalid: TSK-1032 trial rounds 5 and 7 refused Codex starts in
`<repository>/.worktrees/tsk-032` and `.worktrees/tsk-031`. The runner's
folder-trust check and Codex's per-path hook trust are keyed to the root
path as well. One task joins this epic, AC-175 serves it, TSK-1032 gains
an edge, and graph v33 replaces `START -> TSK-1032` with
`TSK-1094 -> TSK-1102` and `TSK-1102 -> TSK-1032`. TSK-1094 is a
standalone task (PR 436, approved), named here as an external predecessor
the way TSK-270 is; no other edge changes.

| Task | Change | Builder | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|
| TSK-1102 (new) | A peer whose working directory passes the bounded identity checks for a linked worktree of the fixture under `.worktrees/` is admitted, with the same exact-path trust and hook checks at that path and revalidation at every check moment; every unregistered, symlinked or foreign directory stays refused; worktree Codex peers run under the existing per-trial bypass option (operator decision, route B) | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high with the `cf-security-reviewer` checklist; Grok 4.7 at high where Codex is unavailable at dispatch |
| TSK-1032 (amended) | Depends on TSK-1102: its AC-4 native trials cannot be valid before the runner admits the worktree peer; its text work proceeds | unchanged | unchanged |

- Admission is a set of bounded checks, since the subject can write every
  file the runner reads, git's metadata included: the raw path is
  `<repository>/.worktrees/<name>` with no symlink component; `.git` is a
  regular file naming an admin directory one level under
  `<repository>/.git/worktrees/`; that directory's `gitdir` points back to
  exactly this `.git` and its `commondir` resolves to the fixture's common
  directory captured at launch; `git worktree list --porcelain` and
  `rev-parse` in a clean environment agree; the identity is pinned at
  bind. Names are never matched.
- The checks run when a request is answered, at bind, before every
  startup key, at readiness and on every later poll of a ready peer; a
  failure revokes the ready record, marks the launch `unverified` and
  flags the trial. Polling cannot see a change removed and restored
  between polls; the README states it.
- Under `--codex-hook-trust=bypass` the runner's existing hook content
  check runs against the worktree's own `.codex/hooks.json`, the file the
  peer's Codex reads, at the same moments.
- Operator decision, 2026-10-31 12:52, saved on the status page: route B.
  Under the default `review` option a Codex peer in a worktree created
  during the trial meets "Hooks need review", because Codex keys hook
  trust by the absolute path of `hooks.json` and the path cannot be
  reviewed before launch; the runner never answers that screen. TSK-1032's
  trials therefore run with the runner's existing per-trial
  `--codex-hook-trust=bypass`, under which the hook content check runs
  against the worktree's own `hooks.json` at every check moment; under
  `review` such a peer stays refused. TSK-1102's Outcome, AC-6 and AC-9
  are narrowed to this route, AC-10 keeps the default-option refusal, and
  TSK-1032's record carries the prerequisite. The finish allowance covers
  folder trust only. Route A, an operator-authorized digest-bound review
  of the shipped hooks for a mid-trial worktree, was not chosen and is a
  note in TSK-1102; it can be filed as its own task if ever wanted.
- Dependency note: TSK-1094 landed through PR 436 (merge `95ebdf940`),
  so the edge `TSK-1094 -> TSK-1102` validates on this line.

## Progress report amendment (2026-10-31)

The operator asked on 2026-10-30 for a command or skill that reports the
status of work properly each time it is asked, in any adopting project:
what is being worked on with the right context, the epics and tasks
involved, the percent complete at each level, the lanes a coordinator
runs with their overall, the order of work, the expected time to
completion and the issues per item, presented visually with supporting
text on `cf-present`; and for Anthropic's artifact guidance to be compared
with `cf-present` and its good parts adopted. The operator's clarification
of the same day (20:15, relayed by the coordinator) set the governing
principle for `cf-present` and `cf-docs-portal` alike: information that is
immediately obvious, visual plus supporting text with a leading anchor and
an order that spares the reader from connecting the dots; a table is not
banned, it is presented visually (colour encodings, inline bars, status
marks, grouping, emphasis) when a table is the better shape; and the
principle is the acceptance bar, in checkable terms, of the planned tasks.
The operator's feedback of the same day (20:40, on a hand-built report)
added: timing reads at a glance on a real time axis with now, the windows
and the dependencies; work landed on the line counts as done while the
report shows what is on `main`, what is not and when it gets there; a
detail section opens to content presented as well as the first path
(layering information, never sweeping text into a box); a needed table is
elegant and restrained; and a report answers the questions its reader
brings, in order. The coordinator settled the command shape on the same
day: extend `codeflow status` rather than add a verb. No open task covered
the report; the cf-present method (TSK-445) and the portal page order
(TSK-446) covered part of the bar. Seven tasks are new, TSK-446 is amended,
TSK-312 is merged into TSK-1095 and cancelled, AC-190 to AC-194 are
added, and graph v35 (after PR 449's v34, which lands first) adds the
edges below and drops `TSK-311 -> TSK-312`.
AC-116's evidence names TSK-1095 in TSK-312's place and TSK-446 AC-4
narrows its table advisory to plain tables; no other existing criterion
or edge changes.

What is on this line for `cf-present` and `cf-docs-portal`, checked on
2026-10-30 at tip `59f134771`: the skill content and `crates/codeflow-present`
are byte-identical with `main` (PR 396 carried the line into `main` as the
checkpoint); TSK-441, TSK-443 and TSK-444 are complete; TSK-445 is in draft
PR 344 (head `201b471eb`, waiting on its AC-7 qualification run); TSK-446
is todo with no pull request. The rendered design falls short of the
principle in four places the completed tasks do not reach: a present
`table` cell is a string drawn as plain text
(`crates/codeflow-present/src/render.rs:406-425`), the present `status`
block tells pass from fail and pending from not-run by colour alone
(`render.rs:425-440`, the `.state-mark` rules), the portal's generated
tables print state as text (`assets/docs-portal/starter/scripts/adapter.mjs:422`),
and no figure family draws time, so expected windows had no carrier
(`figure-grammar.md` section 1; `explanation-method.md` stage 3).

The command, its flags and the skill, named here for the operator's
confirmation:

```text
  codeflow status                       text view, unchanged
  codeflow status --task TSK-NNN        scope: one task, its epic, the line
                  --epic EPC-NNN        scope: one epic (repeatable)
                  --line <branch>       scope: every epic on a line
     ... --json                         the versioned fact model  (TSK-1095)
     ... --present <path>               the cf-present document   (TSK-1098)
     ... --md                           the plain-text form        (TSK-1098)
     ... --session <file>               the session's lanes, seats, verdicts
                                        and blockers, labelled   (TSK-1095)
  /cf-status                            the skill: scope, session file,
                                        context, open, reply     (TSK-1099)
```

Figure: one command carries the facts and both renderings; the skill adds
only what the session knows.

| Task | Outcome | Size | Builder | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TSK-1095 (new) | `codeflow status --json` with scope flags gives percent per level on a printed stage ladder, each item's location and the path to `main` with its window, order with the critical path, lanes with session facts, windows per scenario from now with bases, and issues with check times; absorbs TSK-312 | large | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high | Claude Opus 5.5 at high |
| TSK-1096 (new) | Present and portal tables encode state, extent, tone and grouping from one grammar module, restrained; the `status` block meets the two-channel rule; disclosures accept encoded tables and figures | medium | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high |
| TSK-1097 (new) | Both skills state the immediately obvious bar as nine checkable items (disclosures open to visuals, tables restrained, reader-question order included); the adopted artifact-guidance items are in the kit, the checks and the figure guidance | medium | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high |
| TSK-1100 (new) | The `schedule` figure family draws windows on a time axis with now, dependencies, lane or location groups and the milestones of the path to `main`, on both surfaces | medium | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high |
| TSK-1098 (new) | `codeflow status --present` renders the figure-led progress document in the reader's question order with location layers and the schedule; `--md` prints the plain-text form | medium | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high |
| TSK-1099 (new) | The `cf-status` skill answers every status, hourly and hand-off request from the command at the standard and full tiers, writing its session facts to the `--session` file | medium | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high |
| TSK-446 (amended) | gains AC-9, the bar applied to the rewritten pages, and the edge TSK-1097 -> TSK-446 | | unchanged | unchanged |
| TSK-312 (cancelled) | merged into TSK-1095 on 2026-10-31; its criteria are TSK-1095 AC-2, AC-7 and AC-11; TSK-311 AC-4 reads its schema test as TSK-1095's | | | |

## Dashboard shell and reuse amendment (2026-10-31)

The operator read the hand-built 4.0.0 status page on 2026-10-31 and asked
for proper structure: a report page that reports its aspects by choosing a
tab or the like, a proper side menu like a dashboard with drill-downs, a
task shown with its context when needed; reuse where it makes sense, each
time a report on a topic is presented, so tokens and effort are not spent
redoing it, with proper revisions or versions; and all of it as part of
`cf-present`, not only for reports, neither rigid nor random, so that
each report is not a fresh design. At 13:15 the operator added that
reports are organised by outcomes first, every outcome drilled into for
its detail (epic, theme, task, evidence), as the primary structure, and
that the design and layout are shown as a clickable prototype with real
data (side menu, tabs, drill-downs) for approval before the build tasks
start. The standing report requirements also hold: the deliverable and
the overall percent; each epic or topic with what it is, done and not
done, the reasoning and the percent; blockers with a recommended action,
why and alternatives; operator inputs as form controls with context,
options, recommendation and reasoning; copyable commands consolidated
into a script that runs from any folder; a good-looking page of short
points. The reference look is the 4.0.0 status page
(`codeflow-400-status.html`) and the design brief revision 2.

Three tasks are new, six are amended, AC-195 and AC-196 are added and
graph v35 gains the edges below. Nothing in the runtime draws a side menu,
a breadcrumb or a view tree today (`chrome.tsx:1718` lists top-level
sections in a fixed rail; `tabs` switches panels in one place,
`document.rs:151`); the session store already keeps a digest per block per
revision (`state.rs:137`, `revision.rs:141`), which the change marks
reuse.

| Task | Outcome | Size | Builder | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| TSK-1122 (new) | The operator approves the status dashboard design on a clickable prototype with real data (outcomes first, side menu, tabs, drill-down to epic, theme, task and evidence, forms, script, change marks) before the build starts; the approved layout contract, registry and view tree are written into TSK-1120 and TSK-1098 | small | Claude Fable 5.1 at high (design seat) | the operator's walk-through; Codex GPT-6 Astra at high reads the prototype against the requirements first |
| TSK-1120 (new) | `cf-present` documents may declare a view tree drawn in a dashboard shell: side menu and drawer, header anchor, breadcrumb, context strip, tabs, drill-down; one layout contract with outcomes first as the primary structure and a registry of view kinds with a `custom` kind; linear documents unchanged | medium | Claude Opus 5.5 at high | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high |
| TSK-1121 (new) | A document declares a `topic`; a repeat updates the topic's open session, the shell marks each view and block changed, unchanged, new or removed from stored digests, `present show` prints a prior revision's blocks for reuse, `present diff` groups by view | medium | Codex GPT-6 Astra at high | Claude Opus 5.5 at high |
| TSK-1095 (amended) | AC-15: the deliverable, per-epic what, done, not done and reasoning, themes, blocker and decision judgment fields from records or the session file (`not recorded` otherwise), task context, commands with the repository path | | unchanged | unchanged |
| TSK-1098 (amended) | depends on TSK-1120 and TSK-1121; AC-10 the outcomes-first view tree (overview, topic views with tabs, item views with context) as TSK-1122 approves it; AC-11 blockers with action, why and alternatives, decisions as `decision` and `form` blocks, the script in one labelled `code` block, short points; AC-12 fact-derived ids so a repeat marks only what changed; `stats` from TSK-1113 where landed | | unchanged | unchanged |
| TSK-1099 (amended) | AC-8 the topic register, `present update` over an open session, the context block carried forward, the revision line in the reply; AC-9 judgment fields in the session file and answers read back through `present feedback` | | unchanged | unchanged |
| TSK-1097 (amended) | the bar gains item (j), consistency: the layout contract and registry, a `topic` on a recurring kind, stable ids, a new kind added once with a specimen; AC-2 and AC-3 name it | | unchanged | unchanged |
